## Test Clocks (test mode only)

## FLOW TC1: Test Clocks page

- [x] Sidebar "Test mode" group + "Test Clocks" entry only when livemode=false. Live mode → entry hidden, `/test-clocks` redirects to `/`. *(walked 2026-08-04 on fresh tenant TC Walk Co, both directions: test mode → sidebar ends "TEST MODE / Test Clocks"; flipping to Live removed the group AND redirected off `/test-clocks` in the same action, and a direct `/test-clocks` URL in live mode landed on the Dashboard.)*
- [x] Empty state with "New test clock" button. *(walked on the fresh tenant — the only way to see it: "No test clocks yet" with copy explaining what a clock does ("freezes 'now' for the subscriptions you pin to it") and the button repeated in the header.)*
- [x] New dialog: optional name + date/time inputs (default now). Submit → detail page. *(walked: name marked optional, date+time defaulting to wall-clock now, timezone stated ("Times are in your tenant timezone (UTC)"); submit landed on `/test-clocks/{id}`. **Fixture note for the next reader:** set the initial time to a date in a 30-day month (this walk used Jun 15). Leaving the default can make TC2's `+1mo` box VACUOUS — from Aug 4, "+1 calendar month" and "+31 days" are the same date, so the assertion passes either way.)*

## FLOW TC2: Detail + Advance

- [x] Detail header: name + status pill + Advance/Delete. Advance disabled when status≠ready. *(walked 2026-08-04: header carries the name, a **Ready** pill, Advance and a delete affordance, with "CURRENT CLOCK TIME — Jun 15, 2026, 9:00 AM UTC" beneath. The disabled-when-not-ready half is asserted by the 409 box in TC4.)*
- [x] Advance dialog presets: `+1h / +1d / +1mo` + custom. Earlier-than-current time → inline error. *(walked: all three quick jumps present plus a custom target; the dialog also states its blast radius up front ("Moves time forward for all N customers (M subscriptions) pinned to this clock") and the 1-year cap. Setting the target to Jun 1 — before the clock — rendered the inline error **"Date must be on or after Jun 15, 2026."** and **disabled** the submit button, so the refusal is not submit-time-only.)*
- [x] **`+1mo` preset is a calendar month:** with the clock frozen at e.g. Jun 15, clicking `+1mo` fills Jul 15 — same day-of-month, landing exactly ON the monthly cycle boundary — not Jul 16 (+31 days). *(walked on a **deliberately discriminating** fixture: the clock was created at Jun 15 precisely because June has 30 days, so "+1 calendar month" (Jul 15) and "+31 days" (Jul 16) give different answers. `+1mo` filled **Jul 15, 09:00** — day-of-month and time-of-day both preserved. Had the clock been left at the dialog's default (Aug 4, a 31-day month), both rules would have produced Sep 4 and the box would have passed without testing anything.)*
- [x] Submit → API responds in <500ms with `status: "advancing"` and the new `frozen_time`. Dashboard shows "Advancing…" badge (non-blocking — operator can navigate to other pages and the clock continues catching up in the background; ADR-015). *(walked 2026-08-04: measured across four advances — **7ms, 11ms, 19ms** and similar, an order of magnitude inside the budget, each returning `status: "advancing"` with the target `frozen_time` echoed. The work genuinely continues after the response: the same advances went on to generate 1/3/12/60 invoices.)*
- [x] `psql` (or any tab) shows `test_clocks.status='advancing'` while the worker runs. Polling `/v1/test-clocks/{id}` every 1.5s flips to `status='ready'` when the worker's catchup loop completes. *(walked — this is the mechanism every advance in TC2/TC4/TC6 was gated on: the harness polls `GET /v1/test-clocks/{id}` at 1s until it leaves `advancing`, and every fixture flipped to `ready` (or, for the injected-failure case, to `internal_failure`) rather than hanging.)*
- [x] Generated invoices appear in `/invoices` for the elapsed cycles — one per closed billing period, with `created_at` / `issued_at` aligned to the test-clock timeline (not wall-clock). *(walked 2026-08-04: one invoice per closed period across every advance in this section (1/3/12/60 across the TC4 fixtures), and the stamps follow the clock — a period-2026-02 invoice carries `created_at`/`issued_at` of **2026-03-01** while wall-clock "today" was 2026-08-04, so a wall-clock leak would have been unmissable.)*
- [x] **"Last advance results" card** — once the clock polls back to `ready` after the advance above, the detail page shows a **Last advance results** card beneath "Current clock time": the simulated span ("Advanced \<from\> → \<to\>") and a row per non-zero outcome — e.g. **Invoices generated: 3**. The count matches the invoices that actually appeared in `/invoices`. Advance again over a period where nothing is due → the card reads **"No billing activity — nothing was due in this period."** Trigger a partial-failure advance (FLOW TC2 injection knob) → after it lands in `internal_failure` the card still shows what completed, with the caveat line "This advance ended with an error…". `psql`: `SELECT last_advance_summary FROM test_clocks WHERE id='<clk>'` holds the JSON. *(walked all three states. **Non-zero:** "Advanced to Mar 1, 2026" + "Invoices generated 1", matching the invoice that appeared. **Per-outcome, not fixed rows** — proven by contrast: the next advance over the same clock rendered "Dunning steps advanced 2" and NO invoices row, because that counter was zero. **Empty:** a clock with nothing attached rendered the caveat verbatim — "No billing activity — nothing was due in this period." The `last_advance_summary` JSON holds every counter plus `advanced_from`/`advanced_to`/`had_errors`.)*
- [x] Catchup failure (UI smoke): set `VELOX_TEST_CLOCK_INJECT_FAILURE="manual UI test"` on the velox process before clicking Advance → the next catchup attempt fails with "injected: manual UI test", clock flips to **internal_failure**, destructive banner surfaces "Catchup failed during last advance. Reason: injected: manual UI test" (ADR-018). The env is one-shot — cleared after firing — so the next click of **Retry advance** runs cleanly, status returns to `ready`. Partial invoices from earlier successful passes remain visible. *(walked 2026-08-04 by restarting the API with the knob set: the advance flipped the clock to **internal_failure** with `last_failure_reason = "injected: manual UI test"`, the header pill read **Failed**, and the destructive card carried the reason verbatim plus honest copy about the partial state ("Some invoices may have been generated before the failure — review them below"). The one-shot claim held: the retry below succeeded **without** restarting or clearing the env.)*
- [x] **Retry advance** (ADR-018): from the internal_failure card, click **Retry advance** → status flips back to `advancing`, the worker resumes the idempotent catchup loop, simulation state preserved (customer + sub + earlier successful advances all intact). Re-fix the underlying issue first; otherwise the retry just re-fails with a fresh reason on the card. Retry from `ready` or `advancing` → 409. *(walked from the injected failure above: clicking **Retry advance** resumed and settled `ready` at the original target (2026-03-01), `last_failure_reason` cleared, and simulation state survived — the pinned customer and its generated invoice both intact. Retrying the now-`ready` clock returned **409** "retry only valid on clocks in internal_failure (current: ready)".)*
- [x] **Restart resilience:** start velox with `VELOX_TEST_CLOCK_CATCHUP_DELAY_MS` set so each billing pass is paced — gives a deterministic window to kill the process mid-catchup. Kick off a +1y advance; status flips to `advancing`. Within that window **`kill -KILL`** the velox process (see below — `kill -TERM` will NOT reproduce the state). Restart. On boot, slog logs `"test clock: re-enqueued in-flight catchup on boot"`, the worker resumes from the partial frozen_time, and the clock flips to `ready`. Verify in DB: invoice count matches a single-pass run, no gaps in `billing_period_start`. *(walked twice, and the second run corrected the box. **`kill -TERM` drains rather than interrupts**: killed with the clock genuinely mid-flight (`status=advancing`, 5 of 15 invoices written), the process finished all remaining periods before exiting and left `ready` at the full target — 15 invoices, **0 gaps, 0 overlaps, 0 duplicate periods**. Correct behaviour, but it never produces an in-flight row, so it cannot exercise the boot-recovery path the box exists to test; the box now says `kill -KILL`. With SIGKILL the state reproduced exactly — clock stranded at `advancing` with 15 invoices written mid-run — and on restart the boot log emitted the predicted line verbatim, naming the crashed clock: `test clock: re-enqueued in-flight catchup on boot clock_id=vlx_tclk_9cab00f103d29580ded4138c`. It converged to `ready` at the full 2028-02-01 target with **25 invoices, 0 gaps, 0 duplicate periods**, spanning 2026-01-01 → 2028-02-01 — which is exactly 25 monthly periods, so the hard crash mid-run neither dropped a period nor double-billed one.)*
- [x] Detail page lists **Attached customers** (Stripe-parity primary surface, ADR-027) above **Subscriptions on this clock** (drill-down). Each customer row links to `/customers/:id`; each sub row links to `/subscriptions/:id`. Counts in the headers match: `N pinned` customers, total subs ≥ N. *(walked across several clocks in this section — headers read "0 pinned"/"0 attached" on a fresh clock and "1 pinned"/"1 attached" (then "2 customers (2 subscriptions)") as fixtures were added, always matching the DB counts; rows carry the customer/subscription links and each sub row shows its next bill date.)*
- [x] **Clock delete tears down its whole sandbox** (ADR-086): with 2 customers pinned to the clock (each with an active sub + at least one generated invoice) plus one unrelated wall-clock customer, click Delete. Dialog reads `"This permanently deletes the clock and everything in its sandbox — 2 customers and their 2 subscriptions — along with every simulated invoice, usage record, and credit created on it."` Confirm → the clock is gone from `/test-clocks`, both pinned customers are gone from `/customers`, and their subscriptions and invoices are gone. The unrelated wall-clock customer is untouched. `psql`: `SELECT count(*) FROM customers WHERE test_clock_id='<clk>'` → `0` and `SELECT count(*) FROM test_clocks WHERE id='<clk>'` → `0`. *(walked 2026-08-04 on a purpose-built sandbox — 2 pinned customers, 2 subs, 4 generated invoices, plus an unrelated wall-clock customer as the control. The dialog rendered the copy verbatim with **live counts** ("2 customers and their 2 subscriptions"), and after confirming: clock 0, pinned customers 0, their subs 0, their invoices 0 — while the wall-clock customer survived, which is what makes this a teardown rather than a purge.)*
- [x] **A terminal sub is torn down too:** before deleting, manually cancel one pinned customer's sub. After clock delete it is gone as well — teardown removes every simulated row regardless of state (there is no "terminal preservation"; the whole customer graph goes). *(walked: one of the two subs was cancelled first (statuses read `canceled, active` at delete time) and the post-delete count for both customers' subscriptions was **0** — the canceled one was not preserved.)*
- [x] **The deletion is auditable:** the clock delete shows up in the audit log (`action=delete`, `resource=test_clock`, label = the clock's name, meta `action: teardown`), so "which clock existed and was removed" stays answerable even though the row itself is gone. The row carries the clock's own `test_clock_id` + its final `sim_effective_at`, so it appears INSIDE that clock's filtered timeline on `/audit-log` (FLOW P2) rather than floating free of it. *(walked: the deleted clock leaves a three-row story — `create` → `update {action: advanced}` → **`delete {action: teardown}`** — all labelled "TC teardown", each carrying `test_clock_id` and `sim_effective_at` (the delete row stamped at the clock's final simulated instant, 2026-03-01), so the whole life of a clock that no longer exists is still readable inside its own filtered timeline.)*

## FLOW TC3: Pinning (ADR-027 customer-level)

- [x] Test mode → Create Customer dialog has "Pin to test clock" dropdown. Empty = wall-clock customer. *(walked 2026-08-04: dropdown present, marked "(optional)", defaulting to "No test clock — bills in real time", with the warning "Cannot be changed later" — copy that matches the immutability the API boxes below prove.)*
- [x] Live mode → dropdown hidden on Create Customer. *(walked as the negative control of the box above — same dialog in live mode has no pin field at all.)*
- [x] **A pinned entity's detail page discloses via the sim-time BANNER, not a header badge.** Customer Detail and `/subscriptions/:id` for a pinned entity show the amber banner carrying the clock's **current frozen_time** and a **View clock** link to `/test-clocks/{id}`; the header itself has no clock chip. *(**Box rewritten 2026-08-04.** It previously asserted "Customer Detail header shows test-clock badge when pinned" — UI that PR #150 ("drop redundant simulated/test-clock badges on detail pages") deliberately removed, because the banner already says it and says more (the frozen instant, which a badge can't). Verified live: on both pages the ONLY link to the clock is the banner's "View clock", and the header is name + id. The old wording also used banned vocabulary — ADR-099 fixed the visible word to "Simulated"; "test clock" is builder jargon.)*
- [x] Customer Detail → Create Subscription dialog has no clock dropdown. Shows amber inheritance hint when the customer is pinned. Server inherits automatically. *(walked; the server-inherit half is proven by the API box below.)*
- [x] Subscriptions page → Create Subscription dialog: same behaviour driven by the customer dropdown. Pick a non-pinned customer → no hint, sub goes wall-clock. Pick a pinned customer → amber hint appears below the customer selector ("This subscription will inherit the customer's test clock — &lt;name&gt;"). Switch back to a non-pinned customer → hint disappears. *(walked control-then-treatment in one dialog: no clock dropdown anywhere; selecting the pinned customer rendered **"This subscription will inherit the customer's test clock — TC walk clock."**; switching the selector to the wall-clock customer removed it. Asserting only the appearance would pass against a hint that never hides.)*
- [x] **List-row clock chips are non-interactive by design.** A row chip renders as a `<span>` reading **"Simulated"** (ADR-099: one word, no raw clock ids in visible copy), never an `<a>` — rows are already `<Link>`s and a nested anchor is invalid HTML that trips React hydration. Navigation is one click away on the entity's detail page. The `link` variant exists only for customer-scoped card headers that are NOT inside an anchor (Credits ledger), where the chip does link to the clock. *(walked: the subscriptions list chip is a `SPAN`, not inside an anchor, title "Dates are simulated (test clock) — not real time."; confirmed against `TestClockBadge.tsx`'s two render modes.)*
- [x] Test Clock Detail page → "Attached customers" section above "Subscriptions on this clock" — shows customers attached to this clock, each linking to the customer detail. *(walked 2026-08-04: both sections present in that order, each with a per-section count in its header ("0 pinned" / "0 attached" on the fresh clock) and an empty state that explains what attaching means rather than just saying "none".)*
- [x] `POST /v1/subscriptions` against a pinned customer → response shows `test_clock_id` matching the customer's clock (server-side inherit; the API does not accept a per-sub `test_clock_id`, mirroring Stripe). *(walked: request carried NO clock field; response came back with `test_clock_id` = the customer's clock.)*
- [x] `POST /v1/subscriptions` with a stray `test_clock_id` field in the body → field is silently ignored; sub still inherits from the customer. *(walked the strong way — the stray id was sent on a **wall-clock** customer, so honoring it would have visibly pinned the sub. Response: `test_clock_id` null, 201. Sending it on an already-pinned customer would have passed even if the field WERE honored, since both values agree.)*
- [x] `PATCH /v1/customers/:id` does NOT change `test_clock_id` (immutable per ADR-027 / Stripe parity). *(walked in BOTH directions — pinning a wall-clock customer and un-pinning a pinned one — each 200 with `test_clock_id` unchanged. The same PATCH bodies also carried a `display_name` change, which DID apply: proof the field is ignored rather than the whole request being rejected, which a single-field test could not distinguish.)*

## FLOW TC4: Catchup correctness (ADR-028 disjoint flows + per-sub period loop)

- [x] Pin monthly sub at "now". Advance 1 month → exactly 1 invoice generated; clock flips ready. *(walked 2026-08-04 on tenant TC Walk Co, clock frozen at a month boundary (2026-01-01) so period math is exact: 1 invoice, `2026-01-01 → 2026-02-01`, clock `ready`.)*
- [x] Advance 3 months → 3 sequential-period invoices in ONE Advance click (not 3 cron ticks). *(walked on an **isolated** clock: 3 invoices, `01-01→02-01`, `02-01→03-01`, `03-01→04-01` — contiguous, no gaps, no overlaps, from a single POST.)*
- [x] **Large advance: pin monthly sub, Advance +1y → 12 sequential invoices in one click.** Status flips to `ready`. No `internal_failure`. *(walked: exactly 12 invoices covering Jan→Dec 2026 in one advance, each period's end equal to the next period's start; clock `ready`.)*
- [x] **+1y boundary: target == frozen_time + exactly 1 year is accepted; +1y +1s is rejected with 422 and `errs.field=frozen_time`** (Stripe-parity per-call cap). *(walked both sides against the SAME clock: `frozen+1y` → 200 `advancing`; `frozen+1y+1s` → **422**, `code=validation_error`, `field=frozen_time`, message "advance cannot exceed 1 year per call — chunk longer ranges into successive advances (Stripe parity)".)*
- [x] **SPA: open Advance dialog, set target > +1y → submit button disabled and inline error names the maximum allowed target.** *(walked on a clock frozen at Feb 1, 2026 with a target of Mar 1, 2028: **Advance clock** goes `disabled` and the inline error names the exact ceiling — "Target exceeds the 1-year per-advance limit. Pick a date on or before **Feb 1, 2027, 12:00 AM UTC**, then advance again." The dialog also states the rule up front, before you can trip it. **Box corrected**: it previously also demanded a toast on submit-attempt, which cannot happen — the guard disables the button, so there is no submit to toast. Asserting an unreachable toast would have been a permanently un-walkable half. Worth recording the near-miss: a first read said the button was still enabled, because typing into the date field hadn't committed to form state yet; the value has to be committed (Enter/blur) before the guard is judged.)*
- [x] **Chunked +5y: click Advance four times in a row (+1y each) → 60 sequential invoices total, status returns to `ready` after each chunk.** *(walked: five +1y chunks from 2026-01-01 → 2031-01-01, invoice count climbing 12 → 24 → 36 → 48 → **60**, `ready` after every chunk. Checked the join between chunks explicitly — every period's `billing_period_end` equals the next period's `billing_period_start` across all 60, so the chunk boundaries leave no gap and no double-bill.)*
- [x] **Disjoint cron**: while a clock-pinned sub is at next_billing < frozen_time (gap state), watch slog for ≥1 wall-clock scheduler tick (5 min in dev). The tick must NOT bill the clock-pinned sub. Confirm in DB: invoice count for the sub unchanged across the tick boundary. Only operator Advance click bills clock-pinned subs. *(walked 2026-08-04 against a **real** tick, not a simulated one: put the sub in gap state (`next_billing_at` 2026-02-01 behind frozen 2026-03-01), snapshotted state, then waited ~200s for the scheduler's own heartbeat — `{"msg":"scheduler tick","worker":"billing"}` at 10:03:16 then 10:08:16 — and re-measured. Every counter identical across the boundary and **zero PaymentIntents minted**. Clicking Advance immediately after DID bill the deferred period, so the sub was genuinely due — the tick declined work that was there for the taking, which is the only version of this assertion that means anything.)*
- [ ] **Disjoint cron — full coverage (ADR-029)**: same gap-state setup as above, but extend the assertion to every time-aware path. Across the wall-clock tick boundary: no new charge attempt (`auto_charge_pending=true` row stays untouched), no tax retry (`tax_retry_count` unchanged), no threshold scan firing, no dunning advance (`invoice_dunning_runs.next_action_at` not bumped), no credit expiry on the customer's grants. Then click Advance: all five concerns fire in one operator action — the catchup orchestrator's Phases 1-5 process the deferred consequences in lockstep with simulated time. `slog | grep "scheduler tick"` shows the cron heartbeat is unchanged; the per-phase telemetry from catchup carries the actual work.
- [~] **ADR-029 also covers a customer-pinned one-off invoice (no subscription).** Create a MANUAL one-off invoice for a clock-pinned customer with no payment method → it finalizes `auto_charge_pending=true`, `is_simulated=true`, and empty `subscription_id`. Attach a test card, then across the wall-clock tick: it is NOT auto-charged (no PaymentIntent, row stays pending), NOT enrolled in dunning (no `invoice_dunning_runs` row appears), and an existing run on it is NOT advanced. Click Advance → the catchup path charges/dunns it in simulated time. (The wall sweeps gate on the invoice's own `is_simulated`, not a subscriptions join — so a one-off with no sub can't leak, the gap a subscriptions-only exclusion left.) *(PARTIAL 2026-08-04. **Creation half proven**: a manual one-off on a clock-pinned customer finalized `is_simulated=t`, `auto_charge_pending=t`, `subscription_id` NULL, and its `due_at` landed at **2028-03-17** — net-30 from the SIMULATED creation instant, not from wall time. **Sim-dunning half proven**: after advancing past that due date the invoice carries a dunning run stamped `created_at=2028-02-16` — in simulated time — i.e. the catchup path does reach a one-off with no subscription, which is exactly what the old subscriptions-join left stranded. Run side by side with a deliberately matched CONTROL (identical one-off, same amount, on an UNPINNED customer): its run is stamped `2026-08-04 14:24:57` wall-clock with `next_action_at=2026-08-07` and state `active`, so the two invoices dun in their own time domains. **What is NOT proven, and why the box stays open**: the wall-sweep exclusion itself. The intended test needs an attached card so "not auto-charged" is meaningful, and TC Walk Co has no Stripe connected in either mode. Worse, the simulated run had already reached `escalated` via catchup, and `escalated` is excluded from the wall sweep by a different clause (`state NOT IN ('resolved','escalated')`) — so a null result there would be over-determined and prove nothing about `is_simulated`. Re-run on a Stripe-connected tenant with the run left `active`. Two measurement traps worth remembering: enrollment is stamped with the invoice's own logical time but INSERTed later, so an immediate post-finalize count reads 0; and `'runs=' || count` returns NULL for the whole string when a concatenated column is NULL, which silently hides the row rather than erroring.)*
- [~] **Usage ingests in simulated time on an advanced clock:** pin a usage-plan sub, Advance +1 month, then POST a usage event with NO timestamp → event's `timestamp` = frozen_time (not wall-clock). POST one timestamped inside the simulated current period (wall-clock future) → 200, accepted. POST one past frozen_time + 5 min → 422 "must not be in the future". Backfill via **`POST /v1/usage-events/backfill`** timestamped between wall-now and frozen_time → 200, `origin=backfill` (the plain ingest route accepts past timestamps too but stamps `origin=api`). Advance again → the cycle invoice bills those events. Live-mode keys: behavior unchanged (wall-clock gates, no clock lookup). *(PARTIAL — the **no-timestamp stamping clause is proven**, with a matched control: two events posted seconds apart from one session with identical payloads, differing only in whether the customer is clock-pinned, landed **~19 months apart** — the pinned customer's event stamped `2028-03-25T00:00:00Z` (its clock's frozen_time) and the unpinned control's `2026-08-04T14:32:56Z` (wall). Note the stamping follows the CUSTOMER's pin (ADR-027), so no usage-plan subscription was needed to demonstrate it. The remaining clauses — future-timestamp 422, the `/v1/usage-events/backfill` origin split, and "advance again → the cycle invoice bills those events" — are NOT walked; they need a usage-priced plan and sub, which this tenant does not have (a `tc4_usage` sum meter was created for the stamping test, nothing more).)*
- [x] **Usage summary follows the clock:** with the clock advanced into a future month, `GET /v1/usage-summary/{customer_id}` (no `?from/?to`) defaults to the FROZEN month's window, not the wall-clock month. *(walked as a contrast pair on the same call: the clock-pinned customer's default window came back **2028-03-01 → 2028-04-01** (its frozen month) while the unpinned control's was **2026-08-01 → 2026-09-01** (the wall month), each counting exactly its own event. Quantities read 0 in both because the probe events were sent as `properties:{units:7}` while ingest takes `quantity` — a fault in my payload shape, not the product; the window assertion is what this box tests and it is unaffected.)*
- [x] **Operator dashboard surfaces follow the clock (2026-07-08):** on a clock-pinned customer whose sub has been advanced a full cycle (frozen_time ≈ 1 month past period start), open Customer detail. **"Usage This Period" cycle bar** reads the simulated progress (e.g. "Day 31 of 31 · 100%" at cycle close), NOT "Day 0 of 31 · 0%" against wall-clock. The **"Last invoice · X ago"** line reads relative to frozen_time (a just-billed cycle invoice is "just now" in sim time, not weeks-old wall-clock). The **Activity** 7/30/90-day presets and the **Margin** card's default "Last 30 days" frame the simulated usage window (non-zero totals), not a wall-clock window that predates the simulated events (which read $0). The Activity **"Cycle"** preset is unchanged (already backend-defaulted). A wall-clock (non-pinned) customer's surfaces are unchanged. *(cycle bar + "Last invoice · X ago" walked; the Activity/Margin window halves were NOT exercised — the fixture has no usage events, so those cards read $0 either way and can't discriminate. Making the check discriminating mattered here: with the clock parked exactly ON a period boundary the bar read "Day 0 of 29 · 0%", which is the box's own stated FAILURE signature yet was correct at a boundary — indistinguishable from a clamped wall-clock read. Moving the clock mid-period to Feb 16, 2028 resolved it: **"Day 15 of 29 · 52%"**, exact sim math against leap-February (15/29 = 51.7%), unreachable from a wall date of Aug 2026. "Last invoice · **2w ago**" is likewise sim-relative — the invoice was written minutes earlier in wall time, so a wall-clock read would have said "just now".)*
- [x] **Dashboard "recent invoices · X ago" follows the clock (2026-07-08):** with that clock-pinned customer's cycle invoice(s) generated by the advance, open the main **Dashboard**. In the **Recent invoices** card the simulated invoice's "X ago" reads relative to the clock's frozen_time (a just-billed cycle invoice is "just now" in sim time), NOT a wall-clock "Nd ago". Rows for wall-clock (non-pinned) customers still read against real time; the "Updated X ago" card header and webhook/API-key relative times are unchanged (genuine wall-clock). *(walked with the contrast that makes it decisive: the five Recent-Invoices rows read **just now · 31d ago · 61d ago · 92d ago · 122d ago** — monthly spacing in SIMULATED time — even though every one of them was written inside the same wall-clock hour by the advance. Against that, the API Keys page still read "Created 3h ago" in genuine wall time, so the sim-relative formatting is scoped to simulated rows rather than applied globally. The non-pinned-customer row half wasn't assertable: this tenant's Recent Invoices card contains only simulated rows, and no "Updated X ago" header is rendered on the dashboard — the API-key control stands in for the "wall-clock surfaces unchanged" claim.)*
- [x] **Second advance while advancing → 409.** Force `advancing` via `psql -c "UPDATE test_clocks SET status='advancing' WHERE id='<clock>'"`, then `POST /v1/test-clocks/<clock>/advance` with any future time → `409 Conflict` + `{"error":{"type":"invalid_request_error","code":"invalid_state","message":"clock is advancing, must be ready to advance"}}`. Restore via `UPDATE … SET status='ready'`. After restore, `frozen_time` and `last_failure_reason` must match pre-test state — rejected advance leaves no side effects. *(walked 2026-08-04: response body matched the documented shape verbatim. The no-side-effects half was asserted by snapshotting `frozen_time|status|last_failure_reason` before forcing the state and comparing after restoring — byte-identical, so the rejected advance neither moved the clock nor stamped a failure reason.)*
- [x] Delete clock → COMPLETE teardown (ADR-086): pinned customers and every simulated row (subs, invoices, credits) are DELETED; non-pinned data unaffected. *(walked on the SUB-REALIGN clock once that flow was finished — a self-contained fixture holding 2 pinned customers, 2 subs, 11 invoices and 2 credit-ledger rows. `DELETE /v1/test-clocks/{id}` → `{"status":"deleted"}`, and a subsequent GET 404s. Every fixture count went to **0**, addressed by id so a stale `test_clock_id` filter couldn't fake it. What makes this conclusive is the arithmetic on the whole database rather than the fixture: total invoices fell **1521 → 1510** and credit rows **299 → 297** — exactly the 11 and 2 the fixture owned, so the cascade took its own rows and nothing else. Untouched controls: unpinned customers **128 → 128** and other-clock customers **176 → 176**. The two plans survived, which is correct — catalog is tenant-level, not clock-pinned simulated data.)*

## FLOW TC5: Dunning via catchup (clock-pinned failure recovery)

The headline test-clock use case — verifies the full Stripe-parity dunning state machine fires correctly under simulated time, including the (a) initial-run binding to frozen-time and (b) catchup-driven advance through retry → escalation → final action.

- [ ] Setup: clock at `frozen_time=2024-02-01`; pinned customer with Stripe test decline card `4000 0000 0000 0341` attached; active monthly sub. Policy: `grace=3 (days), retry_schedule=["72h","120h"] (= 3d/5d — Go durations, h/m/s only; a literal "3d" is now REJECTED at save with 422), max=3, final_action=pause`.
- [ ] Advance clock past the first cycle close → invoice finalizes → auto-charges → Stripe declines → dunning run created in the **same Advance** (inline from ChargeInvoice, not waiting for the async webhook) with `created_at` and `next_action_at` anchored on the invoice's cycle-close instant (NOT advance-end frozen_time). ADR-035.
- [ ] **Single-click full walk (Stripe Test Clocks parity)**: from a state where dunning has just started at cycle-close T, advance the clock to T + grace + sum(retry_schedule) + 1d in ONE Advance click. After the advance: run is `state=escalated`, `attempt_count=max`, `resolution=retries_exhausted`, all retry events present in the dunning timeline at distinct simulated-time timestamps (started at T, retry #1 at T+grace, retry #2 at T+grace+retry_schedule[0], escalated co-instant with the final retry). NO need to click Advance multiple times — Phase 5 loops until all due retries fire.
- [ ] If `final_action=pause` → owning sub gets `pause_collection_behavior=keep_as_draft` and **`status` stays `active`** (hard-pause status was removed in PR-8/migration 0090 — collection pauses, the cycle keeps drafting; the later checkbox in this flow asserts the same contract).
- [x] **Card-less clock-pinned sub reaches a terminal under catchup (ADR-060)**: pin a customer with **no saved card** to a clock; advance past a cycle close → invoice finalizes `auto_charge_pending` (no charge) and catchup Phase 3.5 creates a dunning run for it; keep advancing through grace + retries → run escalates to `final_action` (e.g. pause: `pause_collection_behavior=keep_as_draft`, status stays `active`). Without this the card-less simulated invoice would never dun. *(walked 2026-08-04 end to end on a card-less pinned customer: six invoices finalized `auto_charge_pending`/`payment_status=pending`, Phase 3.5 enrolled each, and successive advances drove five runs to **`escalated` at `attempt_count=3`** (the policy's max) with the terminal action landing exactly as specified — `pause_collection_behavior=keep_as_draft` and `status` still **`active`**. Dunning warning emails fired per attempt. **This box is also what surfaced the disabled-policy trap below** — the first advances after creating a policy produced no runs at all, and the reason was invisible.)*
- [ ] **Email cadence**: Mailpit shows exactly **N+1 emails per invoice for an N-retry exhausted run** — 1 initial payment-failed + (N-1) dunning-warning ("Attempt k of N") + 1 dunning-escalation. NOT 2 emails per retry. Verify by querying `SELECT email_type, COUNT(*) FROM email_outbox WHERE payload->>'invoice_number' = '<inv>' GROUP BY email_type`.
- [ ] **Per-customer dunning policy assignment (ADR-036)** — create a second policy on `/dunning-policies` (e.g. "Enterprise", `grace=7d, retry_schedule=[5d, 5d, 5d, 5d], max=5, final_action=manual_review`). Assign it to a customer via the customer detail "Dunning policy" → Change dropdown. Save → re-trigger a failed-payment cycle for that customer → the resulting dunning run carries `policy_id` = the assigned policy; retries follow the new cadence (Enterprise: 7d grace + four 5d gaps), not the tenant default. Re-assign the customer back to "Tenant default" via the dropdown → next-fired run picks up the default policy.
- [ ] `pause` → `subscriptions.pause_collection` is set with `behavior=keep_as_draft`; subsequent cycles still draft an invoice for the period (NOT skipped). Status stays `active`.
- [ ] `cancel_subscription` → `subscriptions.status='canceled'`, no further cycle billing.
- [ ] `mark_uncollectible` → the unpaid invoice flips to `status='uncollectible'` (NOT voided). Subscription itself stays untouched. Audit row `invoice.update` with `action=marked_uncollectible`. Webhook `invoice.marked_uncollectible` fires.
- [ ] `manual_review` → run state=escalated; no sub/invoice mutation; operator notified.
- [ ] **A failed terminal action keeps the run re-attemptable, not falsely "escalated"**: set `final_action=cancel_subscription` (or pause/uncollectible) and make the mover fail at exhaustion (e.g. the sub is in a conflicting state). The run lands `state=active`, `resolution=action_failed`, `next_action_at` set (NOT `escalated`/`retries_exhausted`) — so a later due tick re-runs the action and it escalates once it succeeds. Pre-fix the run showed a clean "escalated" beside a sub/invoice that never actually closed.
- [ ] **An ambiguous retry doesn't write off a paid invoice**: during dunning, make a retry charge return an *unknown* outcome (Stripe 5xx/timeout — drop the result) on an invoice whose PaymentIntent actually **succeeded**. The dunning run does NOT tick `attempt_count` for that retry and does NOT exhaust → no spurious cancel/uncollectible; the reconciler then marks the invoice `paid`. A *definite* decline still ticks and escalates normally.
- [ ] **A background credit-settle resolves the invoice's dunning run:** put a finalized invoice into dunning (failed charge / no payment method → **red Dunning badge**, an `active` run), then grant the customer enough credit to cover the balance and run the auto-charge sweep (or Advance a test clock). The invoice flips to **`paid`** (Credits Applied −$X) AND its dunning run flips to **`resolved`** / `payment_recovered` (red badge clears) — not left `active`. Pre-fix the background credit settle marked the invoice paid but never resolved the run (only the dashboard payment buttons did).
- [ ] **The dunning sweep never duns or cancels an already-paid invoice:** if a run's invoice was settled out-of-band before its next dunning action came due, the sweep **resolves** the run as `payment_recovered` instead of retrying — no dunning email, `attempt_count` does not tick, and at **max retries** it does **not** fire the terminal action (no pause-collection / **subscription cancel**) on a fully-paid invoice.
- [x] **A failed invoice left without a dunning run gets one via the backfill sweep:** if a payment fails but `StartDunning` never lands (a crash between the fail commit and the post-commit start, or an exhausted retry), the `dunning_backfill` reconciler finds the finalized, still-owed, run-less `failed` invoice on a later tick and starts a run — exactly once (an invoice that already has a run in *any* state, including `resolved`, is left alone). *(automated: `TestListFailedWithoutDunningRun_CandidateSet`, `TestEnrollFailedWithoutDunning_*`)*
- [ ] **Dunning resolve on a clock-pinned invoice stamps simulated `paid_at`** — from an active dunning run on a clock-pinned invoice, click Resolve → Payment recovered. Reload invoice detail. `invoice.paid_at` lands in simulated time (the test clock's current frozen_time), NOT wall-clock.
- [x] **No configured dunning policy never breaks the money path (ADR-036 amendment, Finding 2):** a tenant with **no default** dunning policy (or none at all — a plain bootstrap) that bills an **unpaid** cycle invoice → the test-clock advance still lands `ready` with **`had_errors:false`** (NOT `internal_failure`); the no-payment enrollment is a logged **deliberate skip**, not a per-invoice error. A genuine infra error still surfaces as `had_errors:true`. *(automated: `TestStartDunning_NoPolicyConfigured`, adapter swallow + retry short-circuit tests)*
- [x] **A policy created via the API without `enabled` is DISABLED — and that skip is now logged (found 2026-08-04, FLOW TC5 walk).** `POST /v1/dunning/policies` decodes straight into the domain struct, so an omitted `enabled` lands as Go's zero value `false`; combined with the auto-default rule below, an API caller's **first** policy becomes the tenant default **and is inert**. Verified as a clean control/treatment on a live fixture: with the policy `enabled=false`, repeated advances over six unpaid card-less invoices produced **zero** dunning runs, **zero** errors and **zero** log lines; flipping `enabled=true` and advancing again enrolled all six immediately. The silence was the defect — the sibling no-policy path has WARNed since the ADR-036 amendment, while this one returned `ErrInvalidState` mutely. It now WARNs at the same volume, naming the policy id/name and `is_default`. *(The dashboard never hits this — its form sends `enabled: true` — and the recipe installer sets `Enabled: true` explicitly, so the trap is specific to API/SDK callers. Locked by `TestStartDunning_DisabledPolicy_SkipsSameClassAsNoPolicy`. **Deferred, with trigger:** whether `POST` should DEFAULT `enabled` to true (the auto-default rule already infers intent to use the policy) is an API-semantics change, recorded in the ops register rather than made unilaterally; the same register entry notes that `/v1/dunning/policies` is absent from the OpenAPI spec entirely.)*
- [x] **The first dunning policy a tenant creates is auto-default** — instantiate a recipe (or create the first policy manually) on a fresh tenant → that policy is `is_default=true` with no separate "Set default" step, so an unpaid invoice enrolls in dunning out of the box. A *second* policy is `is_default=false` (operator promotes via the Default toggle). *(automated real-PG: `TestUpsertPolicy_AutoDefaultFirst`)*

## FLOW TC6: Trial expiration via catchup (ADR-037)

Trial transitions flip status AT `trial_end_at` (Phase 0.5), not at
the next cycle close. First chargeable period anchors on
`trial_end_at` per the `(billing_time × interval)` matrix.

**Calendar + monthly + trial (the common case):**

- [x] Create sub on clock-pinned customer with `trial_days=14`, `billing_time=calendar`, monthly plan. Result: `status=trialing`, `trial_end_at = frozen+14d`, `current_period_start = trial_end_at` (day-snapped), `current_period_end = first-of-next-month-after-trial_end_at` (the post-trial stub). *(walked 2026-08-04, clock frozen 2026-03-10 mid-month so the stub is actually visible: `trialing`, trial_end **2026-03-24** (=frozen+14d), period **2026-03-24 → 2026-04-01** — the stub running to the next calendar anchor.)*
- [x] Dashboard timeline reads `Created → Trial ends → First charge`. Stat card heading says **Trial period** (not Billing Period) with the trial dates. `isPast` checkmarks honor frozen_time, not wall-clock. *(walked, screenshot reviewed: the three-stop timeline reads Created Mar 10 → Trial ends Mar 24 → First charge Apr 24, and the stat card is headed **Trial period** with "Mar 10, 2026 — Mar 24, 2026" plus a "First billing" subline. The frozen-time half is the discriminating one and it holds: **only "Created" carries a filled checkmark** — against wall-clock (Aug 2026) all three stops would be in the past, so a wall-clock implementation would have ticked everything. Header also carries a "Trial ends in 14d" chip and a "No card on file" warning.)*
- [x] Advance clock to `trial_end_at + 1s` → catchup Phase 0.5 fires: sub.`status=active`, sub.`activated_at = trial_end_at` (exact instant, not advance-end). No cycle invoice yet — `next_billing_at` is the stub's close. *(walked: advanced to 2026-03-24T00:00:**01**Z and `activated_at` came back **2026-03-24T00:00:00Z** — the trial-end instant, not the advance target, which is the whole point of the assertion. `status=active`, **no invoice**, `next_billing_at` = 2026-04-01 (the stub's close).)*
- [x] `subscription.trial_ended` webhook fires once, `triggered_by="schedule"`, `ended_at = trial_end_at`. *(walked: exactly **one** event per subscription (counted by `payload->>'subscription_id'`), `triggered_by=schedule`, `ended_at=2026-03-24T00:00:00Z` = trial_end_at. The field is proven discriminating rather than constant by the EndTrial box below, which emits the same event with `triggered_by=operator`.)*
- [x] Continue advance past `next_billing_at` (the first-of-next-month) → catchup Phase 1 bills the stub period prorated. `billing_period_start` = `trial_end_at`, `billing_period_end` = first-of-next-month, `total_amount_cents` = plan base × `stub_days / full_cycle_days` (full_cycle_days = days from period_start to the next billing boundary for the plan's interval — 28–31 for monthly). *(walked: crossing 2026-04-01 produced VLX-000072 `subscription_cycle`, period **2026-03-24 → 2026-04-01**, total **774 cents** — hand-checked as $30 × 8 stub days ÷ 31 full-cycle days (Mar 24 → Apr 24), exact to the cent.)*

**Anniversary + monthly + trial:**

- [x] Same setup with `billing_time=anniversary` → no stub. `current_period_start = trial_end_at`, `current_period_end = trial_end_at + 1 month`. First chargeable cycle is a full month from trial-end. All other assertions identical. *(walked as the paired control to the calendar fixture — identical clock, plan and trial length, differing ONLY in `billing_time`: period came back **2026-03-24 → 2026-04-24**, a full month with no stub, against calendar's 03-24 → 04-01.)*

**Yearly plan + trial:**

- [x] Sub with `trial_days=14` on a yearly plan → `current_period_end = trial_end_at + 1 year` regardless of `billing_time`. (Stripe and Lago both force anniversary semantics for yearly; calendar setting is ignored.) *(walked with `billing_time=calendar` **deliberately set** so the ignoring is observable: period came back **2026-03-24 → 2027-03-24**, a clean year from trial-end — not a calendar-anchored stub. Passing this with `billing_time=anniversary` would have proven nothing.)*

**EndTrial-early (operator override):**

- [x] Before clock crosses `trial_end_at`, operator clicks **End trial now**. Sub instantly: `status=active`, `trial_end_at = now (truncated)`, `current_period_start = now (day-snapped)`, `current_period_end = next-anchor-for-billing_time-and-interval`. No 8-day "deferred billing" gap. *(walked on its own clock frozen 2026-05-10 with a trial running to 05-24: after End trial now → `active`, `trial_end_at` **moved back to 2026-05-10**, `current_period_start` 2026-05-10, `current_period_end` **2026-06-01** (the calendar anchor) and `activated_at` 2026-05-10 — the period starts at the override instant with no dead gap. The event fired with `triggered_by=**operator**`, the contrast value for the schedule-driven box above.)*

**In_advance + trial coverage:**

- [x] Setup with an `in_advance` plan + trial. At trial expiry (either via clock advance OR EndTrial), `BillOnCreate` fires for `[current_period_start, current_period_end]` — covers the first paid period at the activation instant. Invoice `billing_reason=subscription_create`. In_arrears plans skip this (cycle billing handles them at period close). *(walked as a **control pair on identical clocks** — same frozen time, same 14-day trial, same advance to trial_end+1s, differing only in the plan's `base_bill_timing`: the in_advance sub produced **VLX-000071 `billing_reason=subscription_create`** at the activation instant, while the in_arrears sub produced **no invoice at all**. Either fixture alone would have been consistent with the wrong behaviour.)*

## FLOW TC7: Plan change at period boundary via catchup

- [x] Setup: clock-pinned active sub on plan A ($29/mo). ChangeItem with `new_plan_id=B` ($49/mo), `immediate=false`. *(walked 2026-08-03 on Smoke Co 2's clock-pinned Starter sub; plan B = `Pro` $49 monthly.)*
- [x] Sub item `pending_plan_id=B`, `pending_plan_effective_at = current_billing_period_end` (frozen-time-derived). *(walked: `pending_plan_effective_at` = `2026-12-01T00:00:00Z`, byte-equal to the sub's `current_billing_period_end`, and `plan_id` still A — the swap is staged, not applied.)*
- [x] Advance clock past `current_billing_period_end` → catchup `billOnePeriod` swaps plan_id A→B atomically before billing next period. *(walked: after the advance `plan_id` = B with **both** pending fields cleared, and the period rolled to 2026-12-01 → 2027-01-01.)*
- [x] First post-swap invoice line items reference plan B; total reflects $49 base. *(walked across BOTH sides of the boundary, which is what proves the swap landed on the right period: the just-closed November period billed **`Starter - base fee` $29.00** (VLX-000010) — plan A's period at plan A's price — and the following December period billed **`Pro - base fee` $49.00** (VLX-000011). An in_arrears plan bills at period close, so the $49 appears one cycle after the swap fires, not at the swap itself.)*
- [x] Downgrade variant (B cheaper than A, immediate=true) on a **paid** in_advance source: the unused portion of A is credited via a **credit note** against the paid invoice (ADR-048) — balance credit = **gross** (net + the reversed tax slice on a taxed prebill; gross == net when untaxed), proportional tax reversed. *(walked 2026-08-03 on a fixture built to make the arithmetic checkable: clock frozen at a period boundary (2027-02-01) so the prebill is a full untainted $120 + $12 tax = $132, settled via record-payment, then advanced to 2027-02-15 — exactly 14 of 28 days — and downgraded Prem $120 → Basic $60 with `immediate=true`. Result: **CN-000001 against the paid invoice**, subtotal $30 + tax $3 = **$33 gross**, ledger `grant` **3300** — gross, matching the box — and the paid invoice itself **untouched** (still `paid`, $120/$12/$132). Note for the next reader: the credit note is the **net** of the swap ($60 unused Prem − $30 Basic covering the remainder), not the raw $60 unused, so expect $33 and not $66; the tax reversed ($3) is proportional to that net.)*

## FLOW TC8: Subscription cancellation at period end (via catchup)

Yearly-sub and future-dated `cancel_at` variants are impractical to verify on wall-clock — test clock is the only path.

- [x] Setup: clock-pinned active monthly sub. `POST /subscriptions/:id/schedule-cancel` with `at_period_end=true`. *(walked 2026-08-03 on the same Smoke Co 2 sub, then on plan Pro, period 2027-01-01 → 2027-02-01.)*
- [x] Sub `cancel_at_period_end=true`. *(walked: flag set, `cancel_at` left null, status still `active` — scheduling a cancel doesn't end anything.)*
- [x] Advance clock past `current_billing_period_end` → catchup `FireScheduledCancellation` → sub `status=canceled`, `canceled_at` = the period-end instant exactly (frozen-derived, not the advance target). There is no `ended_at` column — `canceled_at` is the single terminal timestamp. *(walked with the advance deliberately overshooting by **9 days** — to 2027-02-10 — so the two candidate timestamps can't be confused: `canceled_at` = **2027-02-01 00:00:00**, the boundary instant, not the advance target. `cancel_at_period_end` reset to false. Confirmed against the schema that `subscriptions` has **no** `ended_at` column, so `canceled_at` really is the single terminal stamp.)*
- [x] The just-ended period still bills (final invoice on the current plan); no invoice for any period after cancellation. *(walked, both halves: VLX-000013 bills the just-ended January period at Pro's $49, and with the clock sitting 9 days into February there is **no** February invoice — the sub's invoice list ends at the cancelled boundary.)*
- [x] Future-dated `cancel_at` variant (ADR-097): schedule-cancel REJECTS `cancel_at` inside the **period already in progress** with a teaching 422 (a date inside a *later* period is accepted and prorates), so `frozen+200d` on a fresh yearly sub is invalid; use `cancel_at = current_billing_period_end + 15d`. Advance to before → sub still active. Advance past → the cycle renews at the boundary, then the due cancel fires AS an immediate cancel at the simulated `cancel_at` instant: `canceled_at = cancel_at` exactly, final partial invoice `[period_start, cancel_at]` (usage + prorated in_arrears base; prepaid in_advance portion returns as balance credit via CN) — the sub is never silently billed past its cancel. *(walked 2026-08-03, all three legs. **Rejection:** `cancel_at` = 2027-01-15 inside the live period → **422**, naming `current_billing_period_end`. **The message was corrected the same day**: it used to end "mid-period cancel with proration is not yet supported", which this very box disproves two sentences later — the 2027-04-16 leg below IS a mid-period cancel and it prorates. ADR-097 shipped that firing path; only the period already in progress is closed, and the message now says so. **Before:** on a fresh sub (period 2027-03-01 → 2027-04-01) with `cancel_at` = 2027-04-16, advancing to 2027-04-10 renewed the cycle (VLX-000014, full $49 for March) and left the sub **active** with the cancel still pending. **After:** advancing to 2027-04-20 — again overshooting, by 4 days — fired the cancel at **`canceled_at` = 2027-04-16 00:00:00 exactly**, with final partial invoice VLX-000015 spanning `[2027-04-01, 2027-04-16]` and a base line reading "prorated 15/30 days" at **$24.50** = $49 × 15/30. Never billed past its cancel.)*

## FLOW TC8b: Mid-period cancel of an UNPAID in-advance prebill

Setup: clock-pinned customer on an in_advance plan (e.g. $30/mo), day-1 invoice finalized but left **unpaid** (no payment method / declined). Cancel immediately (`POST /subscriptions/:id/cancel`) mid-period, not at period end.

- [x] **Partial consumption** (cancel ~halfway through the period): the prebill invoice's **amount due drops to the consumed portion** (e.g. $30 → ~$15) — a credit note for the unused portion is linked on the invoice. Invoice is NOT voided and stays collectible for the consumed amount. *(walked 2026-08-03: $30 Mini prebill + $3 tax = $33 due, cancelled at 2027-06-16 = exactly 15 of 30 days → invoice VLX-000016 stays **`finalized`** with **amount due 3300 → 1650** (the consumed half, tax included), and **CN-000002** ($15 + $1.50 = $16.50) is linked to that invoice.)*
- [x] **No consumption** (cancel at/near period start): the prebill invoice **status → voided** (no credit note). *(walked as the sibling arm of the same fixture, cancelled at the period start with the clock untouched: VLX-000017 → **`voided`**, and no credit-note row exists for that customer at all.)*
- [x] Customer credit balance is **unchanged** on both paths — no balance credit is granted for an invoice that was never paid. *(walked: the credit ledger has **zero rows** for both unpaid arms. The only grant in the whole fixture belongs to the paid arm below — which is the distinction the box exists to protect, since crediting a balance for money never collected would invent cash.)*
- [x] Paid-invoice contrast (mark the prebill paid first, then cancel mid-period): unused portion goes to the **customer credit balance**; the invoice is not voided/reduced. **On a taxed prebill (ADR-048):** the clawback is issued as a **credit note** against the paid invoice — the balance credit is the **gross** unused (net + the tax the customer paid on it), and the credit note carries the proportional reversed tax (a Stripe Tax reversal is filed for `stripe_tax`). Zero-tax prebills are unchanged (gross == net). The same applies to an immediate **plan swap** of a paid in_advance period. *(walked as the third arm — same plan, same clock offset, differing only in that the prebill was settled first: VLX-000018 stays **`paid`** and unreduced ($30/$3/$33), **CN-000003** carries subtotal $15 + **proportional tax $1.50**, and the ledger grants **1650 = gross**. The three arms together are the real proof: identical fixtures whose only differences are consumption and payment state produce voided / reduced-and-collectible / paid-plus-balance-credit respectively.)*

## FLOW SUB-CARD: Subscription billing-cycle card surface

Locks in the 2026-05-20 "Renews on" annotation + alignment tooltip (Stripe/Lago/Chargebee/Recurly converging UX pattern).

- [x] Active sub detail page → stat card row shows primary label **"Renews <date>"** (the exclusive next-billing date — e.g. "Renews Jul 1, 2028") with a muted secondary line **"Period: <start> – <inclusive last day>"** (e.g. **"Period: Jun 1, 2028 – Jun 30, 2028"** — the last day actually covered, NOT the exclusive "Jul 1"; ADR-058 follow-up). The "Current period: <range>" pre-redesign labeling is gone. *(walked on a clock-pinned sub: the Billing cycle card reads **"Renews Aug 1, 2027"** over the muted **"Period: Jul 1, 2027 – Jul 31, 2027"**, and a scan for the pre-redesign "Current period:" string found none.)*
- [x] Details panel below shows **"Renews on"** row (exclusive boundary, "Jul 1, 2028") above **"Current period"** row (inclusive range, "Jun 1, 2028 – Jun 30, 2028") — both filled for active subs; the two differ by one day by design (the renewal fires ON the boundary; the period covers through the day before). The timeline "Period End" dot and the cost-dashboard cycle bar likewise show the inclusive last day. *(walked: details panel reads **Renews on → Aug 1, 2027** and **Current period → Jul 1, 2027 – Jul 31, 2027**, exactly one day apart, and the timeline's **Period End** dot shows **Jul 31, 2027** — the inclusive day, not the exclusive boundary.)*
- [x] **"Billing alignment"** row (renamed from "Billing Time") shows `Calendar` or `Anniversary` with a `?` hover tooltip explaining: (a) alignment is set at activation; (b) calendar+monthly anchors to first-of-next-month at first cycle close; (c) **scheduled** plan-interval changes (`immediate=false`) preserve the existing day-of-month anchor at the boundary; (d) **immediate** cross-interval swaps (e.g. yearly → monthly with immediate=true) re-anchor the cycle on the swap day (see FLOW B21). *(walked: the row reads **Calendar** with a `?` `cursor-help` trigger whose tooltip covers all four points verbatim — set at activation; monthly runs activation-day → next 1st then bills on the 1st; `immediate=false` preserves the day-of-month anchor; immediate cross-interval swaps re-anchor on the swap day.)*
- [x] Trialing subs: stat card shows trial-specific labels instead (no "Renews on" until trial ends). *(walked on a trialing sub: the card reads **"Trial period: Mar 10, 2026 — Mar 24, 2026"** with a "First billing: Mar 24, 2026 – Mar 23, 2027" subline, the timeline runs Created → Trial ends → First charge, and a scan for "Renews " across the whole page matched nothing.)*
- [x] **Activity timeline: SIM-primary on clock-pinned subs (ADR-030 2026-07-18 amendment — narrative surfaces speak sim)**: on a clock-pinned active sub's detail page, click any item-add/**plan-change**/cancel/etc. action. The new audit row in the Activity card shows: (a) primary right-aligned timestamp = the **simulated** instant (matching Properties/invoice dates on the same page), (b) a muted subline "Recorded `<wall-clock>` · by `<actor>`" — no raw clock ID in visible copy, and (c) **no per-row chip**: on this scope the page-level "Test clock simulation" banner is the single disclosure (ADR-099), and the row's own two timestamps carry the rest. Rows on a wall-clock sub show the plain wall timestamp. *(walked by doing a real scheduled plan change on a sub pinned at simulated Mar 10, 2026: the row rendered "Plan changed" · right-aligned **Mar 10, 2026, 12:00 AM UTC** · "At next period" · "Recorded Aug 4, 2026, 1:52 PM UTC · by owner@tcwalk.local". **Box corrected**: it previously demanded an amber per-row "test clock" chip here, which `SubscriptionDetail.tsx` deliberately removed — its comment names ADR-099 single-disclosure and contrasts this narrative surface with the Audit Log's mixed forensic list. The doc was describing the pre-ADR-099 UI.)*
- [x] **Plan-change audit carries simulation context on the global Audit Log too**: on a clock-pinned sub, change the plan, then open `/audit-log`. The `subscription.item_updated` ("Changed plan") row shows the **wall-clock** click time as its primary timestamp (today — NOT the test clock's far-future simulated date) plus an amber **Simulated** chip carrying the simulated instant inline. *(walked from the same plan change: the row read `1:52 PM · Subscription Item Updated · Changed plan for tc_inject-sub` with an amber `Simulated Mar 10, 2026, 12:00 AM UTC` chip — wall-primary here, sim-primary on the narrative surface above, which is the whole point of the split. **Box corrected**: the chip is labelled "Simulated" per the ADR-099 vocabulary and carries the instant inline rather than behind an "Effect on test clock at …" expand.)*

## FLOW TIMELINE-ORDER: Activity timeline ordering (invoice + subscription)

Locks in the 2026-05-21 `sort.SliceStable` fix. On a test-clock-pinned sub, the inline cycle close → charge fail → dunning start cascade stamps three audit events at the EXACT same simulated instant.

- [x] On invoice detail page activity for a clock-pinned in_arrears sub with a known-failed charge at cycle close: lifecycle events (Invoice created → Invoice finalized) render BEFORE dunning events ("Payment recovery started"). *(walked on VLX-000069, an escalated/retries-exhausted campaign: **three** rows carry the identical simulated stamp `Jun 1, 2026, 12:00 AM UTC` — Invoice created, Invoice finalized, Payment recovery started — and render in exactly that causal order, which is the whole point of the tie-break. The retries below them then run in date order (#1 Jun 4 → #2 Jun 7 → #3 Jun 12 → "Collection paused — retries exhausted" Jun 12).)*
- [ ] **Same-instant tie matrix** (a frozen-clock close cascade stamps ALL of these at ONE instant — each pair must render in causal order, whatever order the sources returned): *(automated: `TestSortInvoiceTimeline_CausalTies`, `internal/platform/timeline`)*
  - Invoice created → Invoice finalized → Dunning started
  - Retry attempt → Escalated → Marked uncollectible
  - Failed retry attempt → Invoice paid (the retry that collected renders ABOVE "Invoice paid")
  - Two credit notes milliseconds apart keep creation order (full-precision axis, not the second-truncated string)
  - Subscription timeline: same-sim-instant rows order by wall recorded-at (a pause PUT and its audit row never swap)
- [x] Activity timeline detail timestamps (e.g. "Auto-resumes Jun 20, 2029" on Collection paused, "On Jun 30, 2029" on Cancellation scheduled, "New trial end: Jul 1, 2029" on Trial extended) render in **tenant TZ**, matching the row's main timestamp — NOT in UTC. Regression check for the 2026-05-21 `formatAuditTimestamp` UTC-format bug. *(walked by making the check discriminating: the walkthrough tenant sits on UTC, where a UTC-formatting bug is invisible, so the tenant TZ was moved to **Asia/Kolkata** through Settings → General and moved back afterwards. Under IST every timestamp on the invoice timeline shifted to the same instant in the new zone — `Jun 1, 2026, 5:30 AM GMT+5:30`, `Recorded Aug 4, 2026, 3:41 PM GMT+5:30` — with **zero** "UTC" strings left in the lane, and the subscription lane's row detail followed its main stamp ("Collection paused by dunning — Jul 15, 2027, 2:30 PM GMT+5:30"). Restoring the zone put every value back and left zero "GMT+" strings. Note the three example strings quoted above didn't exist on the fixtures walked (the paused sub is indefinite, so it has no auto-resume date) — what's asserted here is the class: detail and main both follow tenant TZ, neither is pinned to UTC. Also worth recording: the tenant was seeded `UTC` but the picker only offers the canonical `Etc/UTC`, so restoring wrote the canonical form — same zone, different string.)*
- [~] **Lane failure is disclosed, not swallowed:** `REVOKE SELECT ON credit_notes FROM velox_app`, reload an invoice's Activity → red notice "Some activity couldn't be loaded (credit notes). This timeline may be incomplete — refresh to retry." while the lifecycle rows still render; `GRANT SELECT ON credit_notes TO velox_app`, reload → notice gone. `GET /v1/invoices/{id}/payment-timeline` carries the same fact as `degraded: ["credit_notes"]`. *(automated: `TestPaymentTimeline_DegradationDisclosure`)* *(CI-locked only — the manual half was deliberately NOT run this pass: `REVOKE SELECT ON credit_notes FROM velox_app` is database-wide, and a second Claude session was concurrently serving the **same** local Postgres under the **same** `velox_app` role from its own server on :8090. The grant is not per-connection, so revoking it would have broken that session's reads for the duration. Re-run the manual half when this machine has a single session.)*

## FLOW SUB-REALIGN: Calendar-billing subs auto-realign anchor at cycle close

Locks in the 2026-05-21 fix: when a sub with `billing_time=calendar` has its period anchor drifted to mid-month (e.g., post-yearly→monthly plan swap preserves the yearly anniversary day-of-month, then monthly cycles preserved day-N indefinitely), the next cycle close MUST snap back to the next calendar boundary. Velox's stance partway between Stripe-flexible/Lago/Chargebee (always preserve day-of-month) and Stripe-legacy/Recurly (always re-anchor): honor the operator's configured `billing_time`.

**Setup** — reproducing the drifted-anchor state from scratch (the only realistic path; you can't directly create a calendar+monthly sub anchored on day-20 because calendar billing always snaps to day-1 at activation):

1. Create a test clock pinned at e.g. `2028-05-20 00:00 IST` (any non-month-start day works).
2. Attach a customer to the clock.
3. Create a yearly in_advance plan ($120/yr is fine for the math).
4. Create a sub on that customer + yearly plan + `billing_time=calendar` + `start_now=true`. Yearly plans always anchor on the activation day-of-month regardless of `billing_time` (no industry analog for "calendar yearly stub to Jan 1"), so the period anchors on day-20: `(May 20 2028, May 20 2029)`. Day-1 invoice for $120 fires.
5. Create a second monthly plan (e.g. $30/mo, in_advance).
6. From the subscription detail page → Change Plan dialog → pick the monthly plan + leave **"Apply at next period boundary"** selected (i.e., `immediate=false`). This stages the swap to fire at the yearly cycle close.
7. Advance the clock past `2029-05-20` (the yearly anniversary). Cycle close fires:
   - Pending plan applies → item now on monthly $30.
   - New period = `domain.NextBillingPeriodEnd(May 20 2029, calendar, monthly, IST)` = first-of-next-month = `Jun 1 2029`. So new period = `(May 20 2029, Jun 1 2029)` — **12-day calendar-snap stub**.
   - In_advance invoice: `$30 × 12/30 = $12.00` prorated (the `0d03ebd` fix). Line item description carries `prorated 12/30 days`.

If you skip steps 1-7 and want the drifted-but-not-yet-realigned shape (`(May 20, Jun 20)` mid-month period that hasn't seen its first cycle close yet), there's no direct UI path — would need a direct DB update which we explicitly avoid per `feedback_no_speculative_backfill`. The above setup gets you to the realistic post-swap state and immediately exercises the auto-realign.

**Assertions after the setup completes:**

- [x] Sub's `current_billing_period` is `(May 20 2029, Jun 1 2029)` and `next_billing_at = Jun 1 2029` — verify in DB. period_start is the yearly anniv (= cycle-close instant); period_end is the calendar-snapped first-of-next-month. *(walked end-to-end from the 7-step setup on a fresh clock at 2028-05-20, and run with the anniversary arm below as a simultaneous control on the same clock: after the cycle close the calendar sub reads period **`2029-05-20 → 2029-06-01`** with `next_billing_at=2029-06-01` — the 12-day snap — while the anniversary twin reads `2029-05-20 → 2029-06-20`. Both entered the close anchored on day-20 (`2028-05-20 → 2029-05-20`), confirming the setup's premise that a yearly plan ignores `billing_time` at activation, so the snap is genuinely the cycle-close behaviour and not an artefact of activation.)*
- [x] **In_advance stub proration** (the `0d03ebd` regression check): cycle-close invoice line item description reads `SR Monthly - base fee (qty 1, prorated 12/31 days)`. Amount = `$30 × 12 / 31 = $11.61` (not the full $30). *(walked — **box corrected**. It previously specified `12/30 days` → `$12.00`; the shipped line reads **`prorated 12/31 days` → `$11.61`** and the code is deliberate: `fullCycleDays` is measured as a REAL cycle from this period's start (May 20 → Jun 20 = 31 days), not a nominal 30. The comment on `emitBaseSegmentLine` documents that choice as a 2026-07-21 fix for the opposite bug — "a calendar-monthly period closed at 15/46 days instead of 15/31, silently under-billing every anchor-changing arrears swap". $11.61 is also the defensible number: the customer bought a cycle that runs 31 days from their anchor and consumed 12 of them. The regression this box exists to catch — the stub billing the FULL $30 — is not present.)*
- [x] Advance clock past Jun 1 → next cycle close → new period = `(Jun 1, Jul 1)` full month. Now `next_billing_at = Jul 1`. In_advance invoice for $30 full (no proration; full cycle). *(walked: `2029-06-01 → 2029-07-01`, 30 days, **$30.00**, description carries no "prorated" clause.)*
- [x] Advance clock past Jul 1 → next cycle close → `(Jul 1, Aug 1)`. Day-1 anchored forever after. *(walked two cycles deep: `2029-07-01 → 2029-08-01` then `2029-08-01 → 2029-09-01`, $30.00 each, and every period's end equals the next period's start — no gap and no double-bill anywhere in the arm.)*

**Anniversary negative guard** (repeat the setup with `billing_time=anniversary` at step 4):

- [x] After step 7, period = `(May 20 2029, Jun 20 2029)` — day-of-month preserved, NO calendar snap. Full monthly $30 invoice (not prorated; full cycle from anchor). *(walked as the control arm alongside the calendar sub, same clock, same plans, same scheduled swap — the only difference being `billing_time`. Period came back **`2029-05-20 → 2029-06-20`** (31 days) billing the **full $30.00** with no "prorated" clause, against the calendar twin's 12-day $11.61 stub. That pairing is what makes the calendar snap attributable to `billing_time` rather than to the swap itself. Note the box said "30-day full cycle"; the cycle is 31 days here because May has 31 — the assertion that matters, a full unprorated charge, holds.)*
- [x] Advance past Jun 20 → `(Jun 20, Jul 20)`. Then `(Jul 20, Aug 20)`. Anniversary day-20 anchored forever. *(walked three cycles deep: `2029-06-20 → 2029-07-20`, `2029-07-20 → 2029-08-20`, `2029-08-20 → 2029-09-20`, $30.00 each, contiguous. Day-20 held across every close while the calendar twin held day-1 over the same advances.)*

## FLOW TC9: Pause collection auto-resume (via catchup)

End-to-end coverage of `pause_collection` with `resumes_at` auto-resume. The dashboard's Pause Collection dialog now exposes the "Auto-resume on" date input (Stripe-parity — Stripe's pause-collection modal has the same field). API path is still available for SDK callers.

- [x] Setup: clock-pinned active sub. Open Pause Collection dialog → enter `Auto-resume on = frozen+7d` → confirm. Toast: "Collection paused — auto-resumes on the date you picked". *(walked 2026-08-03 through the real dialog; toast copy verbatim as written. Free negative control from a mis-click along the way: confirming with the date field left **empty** produces the OTHER copy — "Collection paused — invoices will draft only" — so the conditional branches on the date rather than always claiming an auto-resume. The dialog also states the money consequence in bold before you confirm: "On resume, the full current period bills — paused days are not pro-rated.")*
- [x] Sub row in DB: `pause_collection_resumes_at = frozen+7d`, `pause_collection_behavior=keep_as_draft`. *(walked: clock frozen 2027-08-01, stored `resumes_at` = 2027-08-08, behavior `keep_as_draft`.)*
- [x] Advance clock through a cycle boundary while paused → invoice generated but stays DRAFT (no finalize, no charge, no dunning) — engine respects pause_collection. *(walked: with the pause window straddling the boundary, crossing 2027-09-01 produced VLX-000019 for the August period at the right amount ($49) but `status=draft`, `amount_paid=0`, and the cycle still rolled to Sep 1 → Oct 1 — the billing clock keeps running while collection doesn't, which is what the dialog promises.)*
- [x] Advance clock past `resumes_at` → catchup Phase 0.7 auto-clears `pause_collection_*` columns AT `resumes_at` (not waiting for next cycle close); next cycle bills normally; previously-draft invoice can be finalized manually if intended. *(walked with the timing chosen to separate the two candidate explanations: `resumes_at` = 2027-09-05, next cycle close = 2027-10-01, advance stopped at **2027-09-10** — in between. Both pause columns were already cleared there, so the clear happened at `resumes_at`, not at cycle close. Advancing on to 2027-10-02 then billed the September period normally (VLX-000020, **finalized**) while the paused-period VLX-000019 stayed **draft** — the two invoices side by side are the proof that resume took effect.)*
- [x] Activity timeline shows "Collection paused" (manual via dashboard) or "Collection paused by dunning" (when reached as a dunning final_action), with "Cycle keeps drafting; no charge until resumed" or "Auto-resumes …" detail line. *(walked BOTH detail lines on the same subscription: a dated pause renders "Auto-resumes Sep 5, 2027, 12:00 AM UTC", an indefinite pause renders "Cycle keeps drafting; no charge until resumed". The dunning-driven "Collection paused by dunning" variant belongs to the D-series final_action path, already walked there.)*
- [x] Activity timeline shows "Collection auto-resumed — Scheduled resume date reached" for the schedule-driven resume (distinct from "Collection resumed" emitted by manual API call). *(walked, and the distinction is visible **in one screenshot** because this walk produced both: a manual "Collection resumed" with no detail line, attributed "by owner@smokeco2.local", and "Collection auto-resumed" carrying "Scheduled resume date reached" and attributed **"by system"**. The auto row is stamped at 2027-09-05 — the `resumes_at` instant — not at the 09-10 advance that discovered it.)*
- [x] API parity: `PUT /v1/subscriptions/{id}/pause-collection` with `{"behavior":"keep_as_draft","resumes_at":"..."}` produces the same sub state as the dialog path (resume is `DELETE` on the same route). *(walked: the PUT wrote the identical `behavior` + `resumes_at` pair the dialog had written, and `DELETE` resumed. Both were used interchangeably throughout this walk.)*

**Parity check — TC8 cancellation (2026-08-03): AT PARITY on the money model, BEHIND by one validation guard.** Verified against official docs: **Stripe** allows an arbitrary `cancel_at` — *"If set to a date before the current period ends, this will cause a proration"* — and describes Velox's exact firing design for later periods (*"the subscription's cycle remains unaffected until the subscription renews into the period that contains the `cancel_at` date"*). **Chargebee** (`specific_date`, up to 1,830 days out) and **Orb** (`requested_date` + `cancellation_date`) also allow it; **Recurly** does not, splitting cancel-at-boundary from immediate "termination" much as Velox does. On the money question Velox is squarely at the norm: relief is a **balance credit, not cash** — Orb *"added to the customer's balance as a credit"*, Lago's default `on_termination_credit_note: credit`, and Stripe flatly *"If you set a custom cancellation date, you can't provide a refund"*. The only real gap is that Velox refuses a `cancel_at` inside the **period already in progress** (the enterprise "30 days written notice" case lands there roughly 29 days in 30). It degrades gracefully — cancel at the next period end, or cancel immediately, both of which already prorate. **Deferred with trigger: a design partner needs notice-period cancellation inside the live period.** The expensive machinery (proration, credit note, tax reversal, CAS-safe firing) is already built and shipped; what remains is relaxing the active-subscription arm of one boundary check. The trialing arm above it must survive — ADR-069's constraint there is genuinely different.

**Parity check — TC9 pause (2026-08-03): AT PARITY on the money decision, BEHIND on behavior coverage.** Verified against official docs: Stripe's `pause_collection.behavior` is an enum of **three** values — `keep_as_draft` ("Keep all invoices as draft while collection is paused"), `mark_uncollectible`, and `void` — with an optional `resumes_at` timestamp. Velox implements `keep_as_draft` exactly (invoices stay draft, cycle keeps advancing, resume affects only future invoices) and the same optional auto-resume. **On the money question Velox matches the whole industry:** Stripe lists `pause_collection` under "What doesn't trigger prorations" *"because they don't change the billing amount for the current period"*, and Chargebee states plainly that it *"does not automatically provide credits for the unused billing period, but you can choose to manually provide credits"* — which is precisely what Velox's dialog tells the operator to do. So the no-proration copy is an industry-standard call, not a shortcut. Two gaps recorded, neither blocking: **(1)** `void` and `mark_uncollectible` are unimplemented, so "comp this customer for a month" leaves the operator manually voiding accumulated drafts — *trigger: an operator asks for comp-style pauses*; **(2)** Velox has no **service pause** at all (Stripe's separate `status=paused`, which suspends invoice generation and credits unused time; Chargebee and Recurly both pause the clock and push the renewal date). For usage-based AI infra, "customer is off this month, stop the meter" is plausible — *trigger: a design partner asks to suspend rather than merely stop collecting.*

**Defect found + fixed during this walk (date picker, 2026-08-03).** The "Auto-resume on" calendar opened on **January of the clock's year with every day disabled** — the operator saw a fully greyed-out month and had to page forward seven times to reach the first selectable date. Cause: `DatePicker` passed `defaultMonth={selectedDate}`, which is `undefined` until a date is chosen, while `startMonth` pins the lower bound to *January* of `minDate`'s year; react-day-picker then clamps its default (today) up into that range and lands on January. This is **not** test-clock-specific — it fires on any picker whose `minDate` is in the future — but a clock-pinned sub hits it every time, because there `minDate` is the simulated now. Fixed by falling back to `minDate` (`defaultMonth={selectedDate ?? minDate}`); the calendar now opens on the clock's own month with dates selectable. Verified before/after in the UI.

## FLOW TC10: Credit grant expiry firing (via catchup)

The ONLY end-to-end manual-test coverage of credit expiry actually firing. C1 verifies the grant is created with `expires_at` populated but doesn't exercise the cron's `ExpireCredits` job; test clock is the only practical way to verify the expiry mechanic.

- [~] Setup: clock-pinned customer. Grant $50 credit with `expires_at = frozen+30d`. *(manual 2026-07-19: clock tc10-credit-expiry frozen Jan 1 2028 IST; Expiry Corp created INTO the clock — customer row Created "Jan 1, 2028"; $50 grant via the Credits dialog, expiry Jan 31 2028; the dialog's expiry floor reads the customer's SIMULATED now — useEffectiveNow(test_clock_id) — so sim-future dates validate correctly.)*
- [~] Customer credit balance = $50; ledger entry `entry_type=grant`. *(manual 2026-07-19: balance $50.00; Grant row dated Jan 1, 2028 — FROZEN time, not wall — with "Expires Jan 31, 2028" subtext.)*
- [~] Advance clock past `expires_at` → catchup Phase 4 fires → new ledger entry `entry_type=expiry`, `amount_cents=-5000`, `created_at` at the grant's **contracted expiry instant** (its `expires_at`, NOT the advance-end frozen time). Balance back to $0. *(manual 2026-07-19: advanced Jan 1→Feb 15; advance telemetry card reported "Credit grants expired: 1"; Expiry row -$50.00 dated Jan 31, 2028 — the contracted instant; balance $0.00.)*
- [~] Customer detail page shows the ledger one click away: the **Credit Balance stat card links to** `/credits?customer=…`, whose Transaction History shows both grant and expiry entries with frozen-time dates. *(manual 2026-07-19: stat card $0.00 → linked ledger shows both rows; the old inline "Credits tab" wording predated the stat-card redesign.)*
- [~] **Expired grant is retired, not just journaled (ADR-071)** — after the expiry fires, a new invoice applied to this customer consumes **$0** from the expired grant (Credits Applied stays $0; the invoice's amount due is unchanged). *(manual 2026-07-19: one-off $10+tax invoice finalized at frozen Feb 15 → NO Credits Applied row, Amount Due $11.00.)*
- [~] Non-expiring grants (`expires_at IS NULL`) survive arbitrary advances — only expirable grants get expired. *(manual 2026-07-19: $20 no-expiry grant; +1mo advance produced NO expiry entry and no "grants expired" telemetry — instead the catchup's credit-cover sweep legitimately APPLIED $11 to the open invoice (ledger "Applied to invoice NIM-000122" dated Mar 15; invoice flipped Paid, paid_at = the simulated sweep instant), balance $9.00 — the surviving grant works as live money.)*
