Every existing 1280-based assertion in this file therefore stays true by construction — this is a ≥1520-only change.)*
- [x] **Launcher overlap at 1920 — MEASURE, do not assume.** On a tenant with INCOMPLETE setup (so the onboarding checklist renders), at 1920×1080 on `/credits`: record the real overlap between the floating checklist and the **Balance** column. If the widget covers Balance *content* rather than gutter, that is the trigger to move the launcher, NOT to revert the band. *(walked 2026-08-04 on the **Manual Test Flows** tenant — setup 3 of 5, so the launcher renders; the walkthrough tenant is complete and could not show it. **The derived ~308px expectation was wrong on three counts, which is why this box said MEASURE.** (1) The launcher's DEFAULT state is a **149px collapsed pill** at x 1751–1900, not the 360px panel — `w-[360px]` applies only while expanded. (2) The Balance column does not reach the band edge: its cell ends at x=1651 and its text at x=1643, because a trailing chevron column takes the remainder. (3) Overlap is a 2-D question and the derivation only did x. **Collapsed (the default an operator sees unless they act): ZERO overlap on both axes** — pill occupies y 1024–1060, rows sit at y 251–463; measured again at 13 rows, still zero. **So the 1600 band is exonerated, which is what this box existed to decide.** Expanded, the panel is x 1540–1900 × y 725–1060: x-overlap with the Balance cell is **111px** (not 308), and with 13 rows **rows 9–12 have their Balance text genuinely occluded** — 42–50px × 17px, and `elementFromPoint` at each text centre returns the panel, so this is a hit-test, not rectangle arithmetic. Row 9 hides a real **$20.00**. Screenshot `launcher-overlap-1920.png` shows four balances behind "Get Velox ready". Fixture: 9 `Overlap Probe NN` customers created through the real API to reach the panel's vertical band.)*
- [ ] **Deferred with trigger — expanded onboarding panel overlays money values.** The finding above is NOT a band regression: the C2b walk recorded the same class at the old 1280 band ("the widget overlays the Balance cell of whichever row is last in the viewport"), so it predates the widening, and the collapsed default never overlaps. It is also how floating panels conventionally behave — Intercom/Crisp/Drift all overlay content when opened, and the operator closes them. Deliberately not redesigning the launcher for a transient, user-opened overlay on a pre-launch product. **Trigger to fix: the first operator report of a misread balance, OR the launcher becoming non-dismissible / shown outside onboarding.** The cheap fixes were considered and rejected — bottom padding does nothing when the content already fits the viewport (the case measured here), and the panel already grows upward from the pill, so "open it the other way" is not available.
- [x] A credit note whose card refund did not succeed says so on the invoice timeline. *(walked 2026-08-03 on **VLX-000068** / CN-000027, `refund_status=failed`, no `stripe_refund_id`, no money moved. Timeline row reads **"Refund failed"**; before this it read **"Refund issued"**, because the row was labelled from `refund_amount_cents` — what was ALLOCATED — and never consulted `refund_status`. Control on the same walk: CN-000030 on VLX-000067, whose refund genuinely succeeded, is byte-identical before and after — **"Credit note issued — part refunded to card"**. Not offline-specific: any card refund that fails at Stripe rendered the same false row.)*
- [x] Three-channel split: $100 CN → $40 Refund + $30 Credit + $30 Out-of-band → Allocated $100 / $100 ✓ → Issue → all three persisted; CN row label "refund + credit + out of band". *(walked 2026-07-30 on VLX-000054 ($200 + tax = $214.50, fully card-paid): the three fields took $40 / $30 / $30 and the line read `Allocated $100.00 / $100.00 ✓`. Issue produced **CN-000021** `issued` with all three channels persisted independently — `total 10000 = refund 4000 + credit 3000 + out_of_band 3000`, `refund_status=succeeded` — plus the matching ledger grant `+3000 "Credit note CN-000021 — Three-channel split"`. One operator action settled through three different rails without cross-contamination.)*
- [x] Sum mismatch: $50 CN with Refund $20 + Credit $20 + OOB $0 = $40 ≠ $50 → "Allocated $40 / $50 ✗" red; Save disabled. *(walked 2026-07-30 on VLX-000052: a $40 CN with $200 in Refund rendered `Allocated $200.00 / $40.00 ✗` and Issue **disabled** — the reconciliation line refuses in both directions (over- and under-allocation), not just under.)*
- [x] CN > amount_due (unpaid) or > total_amount (paid) → error. *(walked 2026-07-30 on VLX-000054: a $999.99 CN against a $214.50 paid invoice → **422 "credit note amount exceeds remaining creditable amount (214.50)"**. Note the wording is **remaining** creditable, not total — the cap shrinks as earlier CNs consume it, so two sequential CNs can't jointly over-credit the invoice.)*
- [x] CN page: stat cards (Total Credited, Refunded, Applied to Balance, Issued); list rows show channel breakdown ("refund" / "credit" / "refund + credit" / etc.); CSV export has separate Refund/Credit/Out-of-band columns. *(walked 2026-07-30: stat cards populated — Total Credited **$450.03**, Refunded to Card **$132.60**, Applied to Balance **$193.75**, Issued 21. The three CNs this walk created render their channel breakdown exactly: CN-000019 **"refund"**, CN-000020 **"refund + credit"**, CN-000021 **"refund + credit + out of band"** — each beside its Simulated badge, customer, source invoice and reason. CSV columns verified in the generator rather than by download (the export is CLIENT-side, so there is no endpoint to hit): headers `Number, Customer, Invoice, Status, Refund, Credit, Out of band, Reason, Amount, Date` with each channel mapped to its own column from `refund_amount_cents` / `credit_amount_cents` / `out_of_band_amount_cents`.)*
  **Corrected 2026-08-03 (operator-observed):** the "Refunded to Card" stat was summing refund **allocations**, not refund **outcomes** — a CN whose Stripe leg FAILED (no refund object even created) counted as money returned to a card. On live data the card read $192.60 while $30.00 of it was two failed legs. The stat now counts `refund_status=succeeded` only, with a subline naming pending/failed amounts ("$30.00 failed — not returned"); the CSV export gained a `Refund status` column so the same lie can't ride into a spreadsheet; and the invoice settlement waterfall's mixed-allocation description annotates a non-succeeded card leg ("→ card (refund failed)") instead of printing it bare. Gating locked by `tests/creditNoteStats.test.ts`. Note the deliberate asymmetry: the refund **ceiling** (`creditNoteCeilings.ts`) still counts failed legs, mirroring the server cap — a ceiling must stay conservative because a locally-failed refund may still exist at Stripe; a "money returned" stat must be true.
- [x] **Issuing a credit note is atomic — a failed internal effect leaves no orphan (ADR-061):** on a **paid** invoice, create a credit-type credit note (credit-to-balance), then `REVOKE INSERT ON customer_credit_ledger FROM velox_app` and Issue it → the action **fails** and `SELECT status FROM credit_notes WHERE id='<cn>'` is still **`draft`** (the `draft→issued` flip rolled back with the failed grant — no "issued" credit the customer never received); `GRANT INSERT ON customer_credit_ledger TO velox_app` and re-issue → succeeds, status `issued`, balance credited once. *(automated: `TestIssue_GrantFailure_RollsBackCAS`)*

## FLOW C4: Prepaid commits (ADR-078)

- [x] Compose a manual invoice with a commit line via API (`line_items: [{description, line_type:"add_on", quantity:1, unit_amount_cents:9000, commit_granted_cents:10000}]`) → draft created; customer balance unchanged (grant-on-issue, not on create). *(automated: `TestCommitFinalize_FundsGrantOnce`)*
- [x] Finalize it → customer balance **+$100** (the GRANTED amount, not the $90 price); Credits ledger shows "Prepaid commit — invoice <number>"; finalize again → 409, balance unchanged. *(walked 2026-07-30 on VLX-000060 (manual invoice, one commit line: `unit_amount_cents 9000` + `commit_granted_cents 10000`): balance 0 → **+$100.00** on finalize — the GRANTED amount, not the $90 charged, which is the whole point of buying a commit — with the ledger row typed `grant / grant_kind=commit` and described verbatim **"Prepaid commit — invoice VLX-000060"**. Re-finalize with a DISTINCT idempotency key (so the middleware could not mask a double-grant) → **409 "can only finalize draft invoices, current status: finalized"** and the balance stayed exactly $100.00 — fund-once holds against a real second call, not just a replay.)*
- [x] Second commit line on the same invoice → 422 "one commit per invoice"; commit line on a subscription-cycle invoice → 422 "only supported on manual invoices". *(both walked 2026-07-30, verbatim: adding a second commit line to VLX-000060 → **422 "invoice already has a commit line — one commit per invoice; use a separate invoice"**; adding one to a DRAFT subscription-cycle invoice (VLX-000020) → **422 "commit purchase lines are only supported on manual invoices"**. Walk note: the cycle-invoice clause needs a **draft** cycle invoice — my first attempt hit a paid one and got the unrelated draft-only guard ("can only add line items to draft invoices"), which would have looked like a pass for the wrong reason.)*
- [x] **Commit invoices are cash-only**: grant the customer separate credits, leave the commit invoice unpaid with no card on file → scheduler tick does NOT apply balance to it (amount_due unchanged); a normal invoice still gets credits applied. *(automated: `TestApplyToInvoice_CommitInvoiceIsCashInstrument`)*
- [x] Credit note against an UNPAID commit invoice → 409 "…void the unpaid invoice to cancel the commit instead"; against a PAID one → a different 409 pointing at commit relief ("use commit relief (POST /v1/credit-notes with a commit_relief block)…"). *(automated: `TestCreditNote_BlockedOnCommitInvoice`)*
- [x] Draw part of the commit (run billing on a usage invoice), then **void** the funding invoice → balance drops to $0 (remaining retired; consumed stays consumed); ledger shows a negative adjustment "Commit retired — funding invoice voided", and a `credit.commit_retired` event lands on the Webhooks page. *(automated: `TestCommitVoid_RetiresRemaining`)*
- [x] Mark the funding invoice **uncollectible** instead → balance UNCHANGED (block stays live — collections stance); voiding it afterwards retires once. *(automated: `TestCommitUncollectible_NoRetire_ThenVoidRetiresOnce`)*
- [x] **Balance alerts**: set the credit low-balance threshold to $50 (settings API); grant $100 (webhook `credit.balance_recovered`), drain to $30 (`credit.balance_low` with `balance_cents`/`threshold_cents`), drain to $0 (`credit.balance_depleted`) — events visible on the Webhooks page. *(automated: `TestBalanceCrossingEvents`)*
- [x] Grant with `grant_kind:"promotional"` + a plain grant → billing drains the promotional block first. *(automated: `TestDrainOrder_PromotionalFirst_NullSafe`)*
- [x] Grant with `grant_kind:"commit"` via POST /v1/credits/grant → 422 (reserved for the funding path). *(walked 2026-07-30: **422 "must be empty or 'promotional' — commit grants are created by finalizing a commit invoice"**. The error names the legitimate path rather than just refusing, so an integrator learns the model from the rejection — and a commit block can only ever exist against an invoice that funded it.)*
- [x] An unpaid commit invoice that already shows an attention banner **keeps its own reason and its own first action**, and gains the credit sentence plus a **Void invoice** button at the end. *(walked 2026-08-04 on VLX-000060 (finalized, no card, dunning since Jul 30, $96.52 due, $100 granted): chip still reads **"No payment method"**, actions are **Resend setup link · Open customer page · Void invoice** in that order, and the message ends **"…the prepaid credit it bought is already available for the customer to spend: 100.00 USD. Voiding this invoice cancels the unspent balance."** Screenshot `exposure-fixed.png`. This is the fold, and it is the whole point: the exposure is a consequence that co-occurs with the cause, so surfacing it must not displace the declined-card/no-card answer the operator acts on.)*
- [x] An **uncollectible** commit invoice — where nothing else raises a banner — raises **"Unpaid prepaid credit"** naming the live amount. *(walked 2026-08-04 on VLX-000076, built through the real API (pay $45,000 → grant $50,000, finalize, mark uncollectible): banner reads **"This invoice hasn't been paid, and the prepaid credit it bought is already available for the customer to spend: 50,000.00 USD. Voiding this invoice cancels the unspent balance."**, code `credit.commit_exposure`, sole action **Void invoice**, sitting under the "Marked uncollectible" card. Screenshot `synthesize-uncollectible.png`. **This is the state that was previously entirely unlit** — written off, $48,262.50 never collected, $50,000 still spendable, and no surface said so (ADR-078 D3 keeps the block live on purpose; the invisibility was the defect).)*
- [x] **Negative control** — an unpaid invoice that funded NO commit is byte-identical to before: no credit sentence, no Void action. *(walked 2026-08-04 comparing three invoices on one tenant: VLX-000045 (finalized, no commit) → `no_payment_method`, severity `warning`, actions `[send_reminder, add_payment_method]`, no credit mention; VLX-000064 (uncollectible, no commit) → **attention `null`**, still silent; VLX-000060 (finalized, WITH commit) → same reason and severity, actions `[send_reminder, add_payment_method, void_invoice]`, credit named. The only variable between 45 and 60 is the grant, which is what makes this discriminating rather than merely green.)*
- [x] The void confirmation opened from that banner says what voiding does to the **credit**, not just to the invoice. *(walked 2026-08-04 on VLX-000060: the dialog reads **"This invoice bought prepaid credit, and voiding cancels whatever the customer has not spent yet; credit they have already spent is not clawed back."** Screenshot `void-dialog.png`. The generic copy it replaces says "any applied credits are returned" — which points the OTHER WAY (credit applied to PAY an invoice coming back), so on a commit invoice an operator would have read it as the customer *getting* credit. The clause is conditional on the server's own `void_invoice` action, so ordinary invoices keep the original wording.)*

## FLOW C2b: Credits ledger readability

- [x] Customer ledger shows 5 columns (Date · Type · Description · Amount · Balance) with Amount/Balance fully visible at a 1280px window — nothing clipped at the right edge. *(walked 2026-07-30 at an actual 1280×900 viewport on C2b Ledger Co (56 entries), measured not eyeballed: headers exactly `Date · Type · Description · Amount · Balance`, table right edge **32px inside** the viewport, **0 cells with scrollWidth > clientWidth**, and no horizontal page scroll. Screenshot `c2b-ledger-1280.png`. **One honest caveat found by LOOKING:** the floating "4 of 5 · Setup" checklist widget sits bottom-right and overlays the **Balance** cell of whichever row is last in the viewport — transient (it scrolls away / is dismissible) and not clipping, but it does sit over the money column on a data table. Flagged rather than called clean.)*
- [x] An "Applied to invoice DEMO-NNNN" row's description IS the invoice link; a grant with expiry shows "Expires <date>" as subtext; an expiry row reads "Grant expired" (machine id only as small mono subtext). *(all three shapes walked 2026-07-30 on one ledger: **applied** — "Applied to invoice VLX-000058" is itself an `<a href="/invoices/vlx_inv_…">`, not a bare label; **grant with expiry** — "Expiry-row probe" with "Expires Dec 15, 2027" as subtext; **expiry** — "Grant expired" with the machine id `vlx_ccl_d9lg6abmaj…` as small mono subtext (truncated by design — the id is deliberately de-emphasised, and the row's meaning is carried by the words). Type badges render per kind (Grant / Deduction / Expiry) and the running Balance column reconciles down the page.)*
- [x] A customer with >50 ledger entries: page footer says "of <true total>" (not 50), and page 3 shows rows 51+ — `GET /v1/credits/ledger/{cus}?limit=25&offset=50` returns those same rows with `total` = the full count. *(walked 2026-07-30 on a purpose-built 56-entry ledger (52 bulk grants + an expiring grant + an applied-to-invoice deduction + an expiry row, all created through the real API): `?limit=25&offset=50` returned **6 rows** (56−50) with **`total: 56`** — the FULL count, not the page cap — and the first of those rows is "Bulk grant #6", i.e. genuinely past the first 50.)*
- [x] Type filter "Applied" on that customer matches entries **beyond the first 50** (filter is a server query param, not a client slice). *(walked 2026-07-30 with a decisive proof rather than a suggestive one: `?entry_type=grant&limit=100` returns **54 rows** — MORE than the 50-row page cap, which is arithmetically impossible if the filter were a client-side slice of the first page. The narrow filters agree exactly too: `entry_type=usage` → total 1 (the applied row), `entry_type=expiry` → total 1.)*
- [x] CSV export row count = the true total + 1 header (the export pages the API to completion; it is NOT the visible page). *(walked 2026-07-30 by driving the export's OWN paging loop (limit=100, advancing offset until `data.length < pageSize || all.length >= total`) against the live API: **56 exported rows = the API's `total` of 56**, so the file is **57 lines with its header** — while the visible page showed only 25. The loop carries the active sort and type filter, so a filtered export is complete for that filter too.)*

## FLOW C3: Credit-note refund handling (ADR-063)

The credit-note refund leg: operator retry, async webhook reconciliation, proactive surfacing.

- [x] **Card-refund recovery — a failed refund retries to a real Stripe refund, exactly once:** on a **card-paid** invoice (real PaymentIntent), issue a refund credit note with the refund leg failing (e.g. the PI is unreachable at Issue time) → the CN **issues** with `refund_status=failed`, and the Credit Notes page shows a red **"refund failed"** badge + a **Retry refund** button. Click **Retry refund** (or `POST /v1/credit-notes/{id}/retry-refund`) → the retry **reconciles with Stripe first** (a stored refund id is `GetRefund`-checked — a stuck-`pending` one is refused with "still processing", never doubled; a missing id is searched by `metadata.velox_cn_id` and an orphan **adopted**) and only then creates a **real Stripe refund** (`re_…`) → `refund_status=succeeded`. Verify **exactly one** refund at Stripe. A **second** retry on the now-succeeded CN → **409** "refund already succeeded — nothing to retry" (no double-refund). The refund leg is **operator-retried, not auto-swept** (money-out is conservative — unlike the tax-reversal sweep). *(walked 2026-07-30 end to end on VLX-000030 (wall-clock, card-paid $87.25, real PI). The failure was forced the way the box suggests — the invoice's `stripe_payment_intent_id` pointed at an unreachable id (psql; no product flow makes a PI vanish). Issue → **CN-000025 `issued` with `refund_status=failed` and NO `stripe_refund_id`**: the customer's claim is recorded and the money leg is honestly marked failed rather than the CN being lost. Restored the PI, clicked **Retry refund** from the needs-attention list → `refund_status` **failed → succeeded** with `stripe_refund_id=re_3TyNsm…`. **EXACTLY-ONCE verified provider-side**: `stripe refunds list --payment-intent pi_3TyNsm…` returns **1** refund (`succeeded, 1500 USD`) — the failed attempt created nothing at Stripe, so the retry could not double-refund. The same shape was also walked on a clock-pinned invoice (CN-000024, VLX-000059) with the identical failed-then-recorded outcome.)*
  **Mechanism corrected 2026-08-04 (ADR-063 amendment):** this box previously attributed exactly-once to "idempotency key `velox_cn_<cn_id>`" — a premise Stripe does not provide, since **v1 keys expire after ~24h** while our own needs-attention alert fires at 72h; a late retry of a stuck *partial* refund could mint a second live refund, and a within-horizon retry could replay the SAVED create response over webhook-recorded truth. Exactly-once now comes from **reconcile-then-create**: `GetRefund` on a stored id (refusing to double an in-flight refund, recovering a missed terminal webhook), metadata-based **search-and-adopt** for lost responses, state-generation idempotency keys for transport races, and a same-identity monotonic guard on the operator writer. The 2026-07-30 walk above remains valid — its retry ran minutes after the failure, inside the key horizon — but its *explanation* was wrong. Locked by `TestRetryRefund` (5 convergence paths) + `TestUpdateRefundStatusAudited_SameIdentityGuard` (real Postgres).
- [x] **Refund status reconciles from the async webhook (ADR-063):** issue a refund whose Stripe `Refund.create` returns **`pending`** → the CN records `refund_status=pending` (NOT a blanket `succeeded` — the create-time fix). Deliver `refund.updated` / `refund.failed` (or `stripe trigger charge.refund.updated`) → the CN flips to the webhook's final status, matched by `stripe_refund_id`. A **stale** out-of-order `pending` webhook arriving *after* a terminal (`succeeded`/`failed`) does **not** clobber it (monotonic). A refund webhook with **no matching Velox credit note** (created in the Stripe dashboard) is **ack'd and ignored** — no credit note is fabricated. *(walked 2026-08-02 via the **signed-webhook fixture** route the 2026-07-30 annotation named — card refunds still return `succeeded` synchronously (re-confirmed: CN-000026's real refund `re_3TyNsm…0IvpnOQi` on VLX-000030 came back terminal at create), so the create-time-`pending` half stays provider-blocked and was already established honest (create records Stripe's verbatim status). The webhook-reconciliation leg was walked against the REAL ingest path — payloads HMAC-signed with the tenant's registered secret (matched by last4 against `stripe_provider_credentials`) and POSTed to `/v1/webhooks/stripe/vlx_spc_148bf…`, exercising signature verify → livemode check → endpoint-authoritative refund admission → `ApplyRefundWebhook`: **(1)** CN-000026 rewound to `pending` (psql — the state an async rail's create leaves) + signed `refund.updated` succeeded → 200, CN `pending→succeeded` matched by `stripe_refund_id`, log verbatim "refund status reconciled from webhook", exactly ONE `refund_status_changed` audit row; **(2)** stale `pending` after terminal → 200, CN **stays** `succeeded` and NO audit row is minted — the monotonic no-op fabricates no evidence; **(3)** foreign refund id with `created` 2h old (past the 15-min race window) → 200 ack, log "no matching credit note (external/dashboard refund)", zero credit notes fabricated; **(4)** age-gate negative control: the same foreign id with `created`=now → 500, "no matching credit note yet (recent — retry)" — the window discriminates our-own-uncommitted from genuinely-foreign instead of ack-dropping both.)*
- [x] **Failed / stuck-pending refunds surface proactively:** a credit note at `refund_status=failed` (terminal) OR `pending` **older than 72h** raises the Dashboard danger alert **"N refunds need attention"** → links to Credit Notes filtered (`?refund_status=needs_attention`); chip reads "Showing refunds needing attention (failed, or pending > 72h)". A **fresh** `pending` refund (normal async settlement) is **NOT** alerted — it shows a neutral "refund pending" badge only (no alert fatigue). The count (`overview.refunds_needing_attention`) excludes test-clock-simulated CNs (`is_simulated=false`). Resolving the refund (Retry refund → succeeded, or the webhook flips it terminal) drops the count and clears the alert. *(walked 2026-07-30: with the wall-clock CN-000025 sitting at `refund_status=failed`, the Dashboard raised **"1 refund needs attention"** linking to `/credit-notes?refund_status=needs_attention`, where the chip reads verbatim **"Showing refunds needing attention (failed, or pending > 72h)"**, exactly one row renders (CN-000025 · D2 Wall Decline · VLX-000030 · "refund failed") and a **Retry refund** action sits on it — the alert and the fix are one click apart. **Sim-gating confirmed by a negative in the same walk:** an IDENTICAL failed refund on a clock-pinned invoice (CN-000024) raised NO dashboard alert and the count stayed **1, not 2** — `is_simulated = false` is in the needs-attention predicate (ADR-086), so a simulation can never inflate a real operator's attention queue. That negative is what proves the alert is discriminating rather than merely present.)*

## Webhooks

## FLOW W0: Outbound webhook endpoint config (2026-07-05)

- [x] **`payment_method.*` events are subscribable:** the endpoint dialog's event picker shows a **Payment method** group (attached / updated); creating an endpoint subscribed to `payment_method.attached`, then completing a card setup, delivers the event (pre-fix the subscription itself was rejected as "Velox never emits it"). *(walked 2026-08-03, chain fully real: the Add-Endpoint dialog's picker renders the **Payment method 0/2** group first with honest per-event descriptions (screenshot `w0-event-picker-payment-method-group.png` looked at — "0 of 37 selected" counter, no clipping); endpoint created subscribed to `payment_method.attached` → W0 PM Walk customer's card saved through real Stripe Checkout (4242) → inbound `setup_intent.succeeded` attached the PM → outbound `payment_method.attached` enqueued in-tx, dispatched, and DELIVERED to the local sink (http 200), with `Velox-Signature` **verifying against the create-time whsec** (`t=..,v1=..` over `t.body`, one v1 entry in steady state). The wildcard `*` endpoint received the same event in the same dispatch — explicit and wildcard matching both proven. Delivery initially took the retry ladder to attempt 3 because of the loopback-dial contradiction fixed in this PR — an accidental live proof that a transiently-failing delivery heals on the ladder.)*

- [x] Create endpoint with `events: ["invoice.payment_failed"]` → **422** naming the unknown type (the engine never emits it — pre-fix this subscription received silence forever). `["payment.failed"]`, `["invoice.*"]`, and `["*"]` all pass; `["nonexistent.*"]` → 422. *(walked 2026-08-03 via `POST /v1/webhook-endpoints/endpoints`: both bad names → 422 with the teaching message verbatim — "unknown event type "invoice.payment_failed" — Velox never emits it, so this subscription would receive silence; see the event catalog in docs/webhooks.md" — and all three good shapes → 201. **The walk's setup found a three-defect class in the URL validator/dialer pair, fixed same PR:** (1) create-time said "must use HTTPS (except localhost)" but rejected `127.0.0.1` — the exception pattern-matched the literal hostname, not loopback; (2) worse, the exception was a PREFIX check, so `http://localhost.evil.com` rode it to plaintext HTTP against a public host; (3) worst, the egress-hardened dialer refused ALL loopback unconditionally, so the localhost endpoint create-time blessed could never receive a delivery — every one sat `pending` with "refusing to dial private/internal address ::1" buried in the delivery detail. Now ONE `allowLoopback` flag (dev-only, from `APP_ENV`) is honored by BOTH layers: dev admits loopback at create and dial (private/metadata stay blocked even in dev); production refuses loopback LOUDLY at create — "loopback addresses are not deliverable in production" — instead of minting an endpoint the dialer starves. `TestLoopbackException_SameFlagAtBothLayers`, mutation-verified at each layer.)*
- [x] `PATCH /v1/webhook-endpoints/endpoints/{id}` with a new URL + `active: true` → endpoint updated, **signing secret unchanged** (receiver keeps verifying). Dashboard → Webhooks → **Edit** shows URL/description/active + the same event picker as create, prefilled. *(walked 2026-08-03 through the UI Edit dialog (screenshot `w0-edit-dialog-prefilled.png` looked at: URL + description prefilled, "Active — deliver events to this endpoint" checked, the SAME picker as create with `payment_method.attached` checked and "1 of 37 selected" — and the dialog subtitle states the invariant itself: "URL, events, and status change without rotating the signing secret"). URL changed `/pm` → `/pm2`, saved. **Secret unchanged proven two ways:** `md5(secret_encrypted)` byte-identical before/after (`87dbc96b… last4:532d`), and — the strong form — the next replayed delivery arrived at the NEW URL with its `Velox-Signature` still verifying against the create-time plaintext whsec the walk saved. The receiver's verifier never had to change.)*
- [x] Instantiate a recipe → its webhook endpoint (inactive, placeholder URL) subscribes to REAL event names (`payment.failed`, `subscription.item.updated`); Edit it to a real URL + active → deliveries flow. *(walked 2026-08-03: `POST /v1/recipes/anthropic_style/instantiate` → endpoint born `active=false` at `https://example.com/webhooks/velox` subscribed to five REAL types (`invoice.finalized`, `invoice.paid`, `payment.failed`, `subscription.item.updated`, `subscription.canceled` — zero phantoms); PATCHed to the local sink + active → a replayed `payment.failed` delivered, signed, within one dispatch. **THE BOX FOUND A TWO-DEFECT CLASS, fixed same PR:** (1) the endpoints list had a store-level `WHERE active = true`, so the inactive recipe endpoint — the one the operator is told to bring live — was INVISIBLE in the UI, along with every paused endpoint, while the FE's "paused" badge sat as dead code; both dispatch paths already filter `ep.Active` themselves, so the list now shows inactive rows (badge `paused` verified live, screenshot `w0-recipe-endpoint-paused-visible.png` looked at). (2) Un-hiding exposed that **Delete was a soft-delete writing the SAME state as pause** — two past deletions resurfaced as "paused". Migration 0168 splits the intents: delete stamps `deleted_at` (gone from list/Get; re-delete 404s), pause stays `active=false` and visible. `TestInactiveEndpoint_ListedButNeverDispatched` pins listed-but-not-dispatched AND deleted-stays-gone.)*

## FLOW W1: Stripe signature verification

Probe payload chosen to be side-effect-free BY DESIGN: a stale-`pending` `refund.updated` for CN-000026's already-terminal refund — the monotonic guard makes acceptance a provable no-op. `t=now` → 200 `{"status":"processed"}`; boundary control `t=now−290` (inside `signatureToleranceSec=300`) also 200. Both events persisted to `stripe_webhook_events`; CN still `succeeded`, zero new audit rows — accepted, processed, and honestly mutation-free.)*
- [x] Replay 5+ min later → rejected (timestamp tolerance). *(walked 2026-08-03: same payload signed VALIDLY over `t=now−360` → 400 `invalid signature`; server log reason verbatim **"timestamp outside tolerance"**. Event not persisted.)*
- [x] Modified payload + original signature → rejected. *(walked 2026-08-03: the accepted probe's exact `Stripe-Signature` header replayed over a body with one field mutated (`"pending"`→`"succeeded"`) → 400; log reason verbatim **"signature mismatch"**. The two rejects carry DIFFERENT logged reasons, so each guard demonstrably fired for its own cause rather than one guard swallowing both. Parity: 300s tolerance matches Stripe's own receiver guidance — AT PARITY.)*

- [x] Backdate `secondary_secret_expires_at` → header carries one entry; only new verifies. *(walked 2026-08-03: expiry backdated one minute (the premise axis — the assertion rides on the header), next replayed delivery carried ONE `v1=`; the new secret verified, the old did not. The grace boundary drops the old key exactly, in the very next delivery.)*

## FLOW W3: Delivery stats

- [x] Endpoints page Success Rate column: green ≥95%, amber 70–94%, red <70%. *(walked 2026-08-03, all three bands observed live on real deliveries — screenshots `w3-success-rate-red-band.png` + `w3-success-rate-amber-band.png` looked at: the walk's failing endpoint at **red 0.0%** (its one delivery exhausted the retry ladder to `failed`), then **amber 75.0%** (3 succeeded / 4 total after repointing + replays), with the two sibling endpoints at **green 100.0%** throughout. Rate definition, for the next reader: `succeeded/total` over ALL delivery rows for the endpoint — attempts are not deliveries, so a delivery that fails twice and then lands counts as one success; a `pending` row drags the rate until it resolves. Bonus control caught by looking: `/pm2`'s "Dual-signing until…" badge correctly VANISHED once its grace was expired while the sibling's (still in grace) persisted.)*
- [x] Replay failed event → success rate updates. *(walked 2026-08-03: with the endpoint at red 0.0%, repointed to a succeeding path and replayed three times → three fresh deliveries succeeded and the column moved 0.0% → **75.0%**, crossing the red→amber threshold on screen. The original failed delivery stays `failed` — replays mint new events + deliveries; nothing rewrites history.)*

## FLOW W4: Live event stream + replay

- [x] **Payment anomalies emit PER-CAUSE events (ADR-068)** — a duplicate capture, an amount mismatch, and a payment on a voided invoice each emit their own event type (`payment.duplicate_charge` / `payment.amount_mismatch` / `payment.received_on_voided_invoice`) on the stream — never one aggregate "attention changed" event. *(walked 2026-08-03 from the recorded event table plus the 2026-07-29 anomaly walks that produced it: `payment.duplicate_charge` ×1 and `payment.amount_mismatch` ×1 exist as rows, each minted by a REAL Stripe-driven anomaly (the CLI-created second PI and the superseded-Checkout amount mismatch documented in I5b/D-walk annotations) — plus a per-cause `payment.currency_mismatch` row the box didn't even list. ZERO aggregate types across 800+ payment.* events. The third named cause, `received_on_voided_invoice`, has never fired live — deliberately hard to construct since #648 expires Checkout sessions at void, which is the product working — and is CI-pinned instead: `TestSettleSucceeded_PaymentOnVoided` asserts exactly one event of exactly that type (emitter settlement.go:118).)*

- [x] Webhooks → Events → recent deliveries with state dot. *(walked 2026-08-03 on the Live Tail page (Webhooks → Events → Open Live Tail): every event row carries a status chip — and **the chip was lying**: `frameStatusFromEvent` GUESSED status from age ("<24h ⇒ pending, else delivered" — a heuristic whose own comment admitted the JOIN was skipped), so every settled event younger than a day wore grey `pending` all day, and a permanently-failed delivery would have read `delivered` once it aged past the ladder. FIXED same PR: the SSE snapshot now derives each event's status from its actual deliveries in one roll-up query (any pending → `pending`, else any failed → `failed`, else `delivered`; no deliveries → `no_endpoints`, which the FE's future-proof badge renders neutrally with zero FE changes). Verified live post-fix: all-succeeded events read `delivered`, the ladder event with its failed delivery reads `failed`, pre-endpoint history reads `no_endpoints`. `TestEventDeliveryStatuses_RollsUpFromRealDeliveries` pins the roll-up against real Postgres, precedence mutation-verified.)*
- [x] Trigger event → row streams in <1s without refresh (SSE). *(walked 2026-08-03 with an in-page measurement: a MutationObserver armed against the replay response's `event_id` found the row **already rendered when the replay POST's own response resolved** — the SSE frame beat the HTTP round-trip, because the handler publishes to the in-memory bus synchronously inside the request. Sub-second by construction, no refresh.)*
- [x] Click delivery → expands the per-attempt Delivery Timeline: per-delivery status chip + HTTP code, endpoint id, attempted/completed instants, error text verbatim, response body behind **Show body**, and the payload's sha256. *(walked 2026-08-03 on the ladder event — screenshot `w4-live-tail-delivery-timeline.png` looked at: "Delivery Timeline (22 attempts)" with green `succeeded`/`HTTP 200` chips, and Delivery #1 carrying the walk's own pre-fix refusal in red verbatim — "refusing to dial private/internal address ::1" — honest history. This box previously promised a "side panel: URL, status, headers, body"; the shipped shape is the inline expansion above, which shows everything except request headers — deliberately: the signature header is secret-derived, and the payload hash + Show body cover receiver debugging. Box text updated to the real observable.)*
- [x] Replay failed → fresh attempt fires; original preserved. *(walked 2026-08-03: replaying the ladder event minted a NEW event id each time (`replay_of` recorded), each with its own fresh deliveries — while the original delivery row kept `status=failed, attempt_count=6` untouched. DB after the walk: 1 failed original beside 3 succeeded replays on the same endpoint; nothing rewrote history.)*
- [x] **Test-mode replay stays test-mode:** in **test** mode, replay a webhook event → the new delivery row appears in the test-mode Events view and `SELECT livemode FROM webhook_deliveries WHERE id='<new>'` is `false`. *(walked 2026-08-03: the replayed event `vlx_whevt_d9o3ti3…` rendered in the test-mode Live Tail, and its event row + all three fan-out deliveries carry `livemode = f`.)*
- [x] Multi-retry event → the Delivery Timeline flags payload identity between attempts. *(walked 2026-08-03: each delivery entry carries the payload's **sha256** and a "**payload identical to previous**" marker when hashes match — the common case, since replays don't mutate payloads; a differing payload surfaces by its hash changing. This box previously said a "Diff tab"; the shipped shape is the inline hash + identity marker, which answers the operator question — "did the payload change between attempts?" — without a tab. Box text updated to the real observable.)*
- [x] Stop Redis → the events UI keeps working; readiness names what it actually checks. *(walked 2026-08-03 — the box previously claimed "readiness degraded; UI loads but stops streaming", and BOTH halves were false for the shipped system: with `docker stop velox-redis-1`, `/health/ready` stayed `ok` (its checks are api/database/scheduler — Redis serves distributed rate limiting only) and the Live Tail kept streaming perfectly (in-memory bus; a replay's frame rendered within its own request round-trip, pill "Live · 52 events"). Box rewritten to reality. The REAL Redis-down consequence is production-only and recorded in the velox-ops register (`redis-down-fails-closed-but-readiness-green`): the prod rate limiter fails CLOSED, so public routes 429 while readiness stays green and the LB keeps routing — trigger: production cutover.)*
- [x] **Failed delivery walks the full retry ladder:** point an endpoint at a URL that always 5xxs, trigger an event → the delivery row stays `pending` across **five** scheduled retries with backoffs 1m → 5m → 30m → 2h → **24h** (`next_retry_at` steps match), and only flips to `failed` after the attempt past the 24h slot (`attempt_count=6`). *(walked 2026-08-03 against a local always-500 sink. The first THREE rungs ran on real wall-clock: attempt 1 at 06:25:36 wrote next_retry +72s (1m + jitter), attempt 2 landed 06:27:06, attempt 3 landed 06:32:40 (+5m34s — the 5m rung) and wrote **+30m** (07:02:45). The deep rungs were tick-phased per strategy §3.13 — each system-written `next_retry_at` RECORDED before the acceleration poke, so the asserted values are the scheduler's own: attempt 4 wrote **+120m**, attempt 5 wrote **+1440m**, and attempt 6 flipped the row to **`failed`** — terminal only past the 24h slot, `attempt_count=6`, exactly the ladder (`retryBackoffs`) the code declares. Status stayed `pending` through all five waits.)*

---
- [x] **A redelivered `payment_intent.payment_failed` notifies once, not twice** — decline a charge on a non-interactive auto-charge so the customer gets a payment-failed email, then **resend the same event** (`stripe events resend <evt_id>`). Mailpit shows exactly **one** "payment failed" email and the webhook event stream shows exactly **one** `payment.failed`. A genuinely **new** retry that fails (a fresh PaymentIntent) DOES notify again. *(walked 2026-08-03 on VLX-000070 — a fresh invoice on the 0341-carded customer, auto-charge declined non-interactively at finalize (real PI `pi_3U0G5G…`, "auto-charge declined, invoice stays finalized; dunning drives collection", dunning started). Baseline: ONE "Payment failed for invoice VLX-000070" in Mailpit, ONE outbound `payment.failed`. `stripe events resend evt_3U0G5G…` → counts UNCHANGED (email 1, outbound 1), dunning `attempt_count` still 0, log verbatim **"duplicate webhook event, skipping"** on the exact resent id — the `(tenant, livemode, stripe_event_id)` dedup layer. The new-PI-notifies-again half is the D1 walk's proven behavior (each real dunning retry sent its own per-attempt email on distinct PIs).)*
- [x] **A redelivered `payment_intent.succeeded` settles once, not twice** — auto-charge an invoice to `paid`, then **resend the same event** (`stripe events resend <evt_id>`, or re-POST the identical webhook). The invoice stays `paid`, Mailpit shows exactly **one** receipt, and the webhook stream shows exactly **one** `invoice.paid` — the redelivery is a no-op (terminal-state guard logs "payment already settled; skipping duplicate success settlement"; the webhook store also dedups on `(tenant, livemode, stripe_event_id)`). A genuinely **new** PaymentIntent that succeeds (e.g. dunning recovery on the same invoice) DOES settle + receipt again. (Success twin of the `payment_intent.payment_failed` row above; the guard differs because `paid` is terminal while failure is not.) *(walked 2026-08-03 on VLX-000069 — auto-charge at finalize settled it paid inline on real `pi_3U0G40…`. BOTH dedup layers observed as their own verbatim log lines: the webhook's first arrival hit the terminal-state guard ("payment already settled; skipping duplicate success settlement" — inline settle had won the race), and `stripe events resend evt_3U0G40…` hit the event-id layer ("duplicate webhook event, skipping"). After the resend: invoice still `paid/succeeded`, outbound `invoice.paid` count still 1, Mailpit receipts still 1, stored stripe events still 2. The new-PI half is the D1 recovery walk's proven behavior.)*

## Customers

## FLOW CU1: Settings + billing profile

- [ ] Settings: company name change → "Saved" indicator. Navigating with unsaved changes prompts.
- [ ] **Settings changes are audited field-by-field**: change e.g. the default currency + Net terms, save, open `/audit-log` → one "Updated Settings" row whose expanded metadata shows `changed` with each field's `from`/`to` (e.g. `default_currency: USD → EUR`). A no-op save (no fields changed) adds no field-level row. Actor = the signed-in operator, not "System". (The `audit_fail_closed` example this flow used to reference is retired — ADR-089 removed the setting, migration 0149 dropped the column; fail-closed is now structural, since every audit row rides its mutation's transaction.)
- [ ] Currency change → new invoices use it; existing unchanged.
- [ ] Edit billing profile (address, tax ID) → PDF reflects update.
- [ ] Edit billing profile when customer has `stripe_customer_id` set → Stripe Dashboard → Customer shows the updated legal_name / phone / address / tax_exempt immediately (Phase 1 Velox→Stripe sync, best-effort, fires on every customer/profile update). <!-- currency-ok: Stripe Customer object's own tax_exempt field -->
- [ ] Create a brand-new customer with email + display_name + billing profile → first PM action (operator send-setup-email / copy setup-link) lazily creates the Stripe Customer pre-populated with email, name, address, and tax_exempt status — Stripe Dashboard shows a fully-populated row, NOT a blank one with only `velox_*` metadata. <!-- currency-ok: Stripe's own tax_exempt field -->
- [ ] Set billing profile tax_id (e.g. `eu_vat` + `DE123456789`) → Stripe Dashboard → Customer → Tax IDs tab shows the entry (Phase 2 reconcile). Change tax_id value → old entry gone, new entry present. Clear tax_id → Tax IDs tab empty. Brand-new customer with tax_id pre-filled in profile → first PM action creates the Stripe Customer with the tax_id already in the Tax IDs tab (no follow-up update call needed).
- [ ] Draft invoice held >24h, then click Finalize → operator sees `tax calculation expired (age 24h0m, max 23h0m) — retry tax to refresh, then finalize` (Phase 2 expiry guard). Click Retry tax → tax recomputes → Finalize succeeds, Stripe Tax dashboard shows a `tx_*` transaction. Without the guard, finalize previously left the invoice with `tax_calculation_id` populated but no `tax_transaction_id`.
- [ ] **Tax-retry flush on profile update.** Customer with a draft invoice stuck on `tax_error_code = customer_data_invalid` (e.g. US customer missing `postal_code`). Edit billing profile → fill the missing field → Save. Without per-invoice clicking: invoice's `tax_status` flips to `succeeded` (or back to `failed` with a different code if still wrong), and `slog | grep "billing profile flush retried tax errors"` shows `processed >= 1`. Other stuck-tax codes (e.g. `provider_outage`) are NOT replayed by the flush — only `customer_data_invalid`.

That read is also what exposed the defect fixed the same day: with `VELOX_API_BASE_URL` unset the clipboard receives a bare **path**, not a URL the customer can open; the missing boot WARN is now in place.)*

Token prefix `vlx_pcd_` + 64 hex; URL is `{VELOX_API_BASE_URL or relative}/v1/public/cost-dashboard/<token>`. *(walked: response carries exactly the two keys, prefix `vlx_pcd_`, and the remainder matches `[0-9a-f]{64}` — checked by regex, not by eyeballing length.)*
- [x] `GET /v1/public/cost-dashboard/{token}` (no auth) → sanitized projection:
  - Present: `customer_id`, `tenant_id`, `billing_period {start, end, source}`, `subscriptions[]` (id + plan_name + currency + period), `usage[]` (meter_key + meter_name + unit + currency + total_quantity + total_amount_cents + rules[]), `totals[]`, `projected_total_cents`.
  - **Absent**: email, display_name, external_id, metadata, billing_profile, plan_id, rating_rule_version_id, warnings.
  *(walked 2026-08-03 unauthenticated. Every listed key present; all eight forbidden names scanned for in the **raw body** — plus the customer's actual email and display-name values, since a leak could carry the value under a different key. The response also carries `livemode`, which the flow's Present list omits — harmless and deliberate, but noted so the next reader isn't surprised. **Added the same day:** this endpoint's `200` had no OpenAPI response schema at all; it now has one, because its `unit_amount_decimal` is decimal **cents** and a partner reading it as dollars would overstate cost 100×.)*
- [x] No active sub → `billing_period.source='no_subscription'`, empty arrays, **NOT 5xx** (200 with the empty envelope). *(walked on a purpose-made customer with no subscription: **200**, `source='no_subscription'`, all four arrays empty, `projected_total_cents: 0`. Worth knowing: `start`/`end` come back as the **zero time** `0001-01-01T00:00:00Z`, not null — a consumer that renders the period without branching on `source` will print "January 1, year 1". Documented on the `source` field in the OpenAPI schema rather than changed, since `source` is already the discriminator the contract promises.)*
- [x] Rotate → previous URL returns 401 `invalid cost-dashboard token` immediately. Audit log records the rotation (`action=rotate`, `resource_type=customer`, `metadata.surface=cost_dashboard_token`); the plaintext token is NEVER in the audit row. *(walked: old token → 401 with exactly that message, new → 200; audit row is `action=rotate` / `resource_type=customer` / `metadata.surface=cost_dashboard_token`. The no-plaintext half was checked the strong way — a `LIKE '%<token>%'` scan over `audit_log.metadata` for **both** the rotated-out and current tokens returned **0 rows**, rather than just eyeballing one row. Incidental control: the clock-pinned customer's rotate rows carry `test_clock_id` + `sim_effective_at` (ADR-090 §5) while the unpinned customer's row carries neither.)*
- [x] Tampered / unknown token → 401 (same 401 as rotated — anti-enumeration). Wrong prefix (no `vlx_pcd_`) → 401 fast-path without DB lookup. *(walked: a well-formed-but-unknown `vlx_pcd_` token and a wrong-prefix token both return **401**, identical to the rotated-out one — never 404, which would confirm existence. The "without DB lookup" half is not observable from outside, so it was confirmed at the source: `handler.go` returns 401 on the prefix check **before** reaching `costSvc.GetByToken`.)*
- [x] Rate limit: 61+ req/min/IP → 429 (shares the hosted-invoice bucket; tighter than the general 100/min). *(walked: 70 unauthenticated requests in a burst → **61× 200 then 9× 429**. The limiter engages well below the general 100/min, as claimed. The exact cutoff lands one past 60 because the burst takes a couple of seconds and a 60/min bucket refills ~1 token in that time — so read this box as "the tighter bucket engages", not as an exact 61st-request boundary.)*
- [ ] Embeddable widget (not yet shipped): a `<VeloxCostDashboard token baseUrl />` React widget is the natural next step — defer until first design partner asks. The JSON projection is consumer-ready as-is; partners can render their own UI today. *(Deliberately left unticked — this box records a **deferral**, not an observable. There is nothing to walk until a design partner triggers it.)*

**Parity check (2026-08-03): BEHIND on the rendered surface, AT PARITY on the data primitive.** Verified against official docs: **Metronome** ships `POST /v1/dashboards/getEmbeddableUrl` for an iframe embed with `color_overrides`; **Orb** ships signed hosted invoice + customer portals showing invoices and current usage; **Lago** ships an embeddable customer portal (Premium tier only). **Stripe** ships API only and expects the vendor to build the dashboard. So the JSON-only choice is defensible — it is Stripe's own posture, and Orb documents the build-your-own path for exactly this use case — but Velox is **not** ahead of the peer set, which is what ADR-032 claimed until it was corrected the same day. Two consequences: the widget stays deferred on its existing trigger (a DP asks), and a **new** deferral is recorded — the projection is **current-period only**, while every peer exposes history (Metronome 30/60/90 days; OpenAI and Anthropic consoles go to minute granularity). That is a *data* gap, not a UI gap, and it is the half this endpoint exists to serve. **Trigger: if the first design partner is AI infrastructure, historical time-series on this projection is the fast-follow.**

## Platform

## FLOW P2: Audit log

- [x] Several actions (create customer, grant credits, void invoice, change plan) → all logged. *(walked 2026-08-03 against the campaign-rich log — every action class exists as rows minted by REAL walked flows, verified by census: `create customer` ×28 (incl. today's W0 PM Walk / I4d Rotation Walk), `grant credit` ×75 (C1 walks + today), `void invoice` ×5 (I4/D2 walks), `subscription.item_updated` ×9 + `update subscription` (B-series plan changes), plus finalize ×60, credit_note ×30, test_clock ×38 — the log records the whole campaign.)*
- [x] **Credit grant/adjust audit rows share the ledger's fate (ADR-090, first in-tx domain)**: grant credits → the `grant` audit row and the ledger entry appear together; attempt an over-balance deduction (400) → NO `credit.deduction` audit row exists for the failed attempt (phantom class dead). Same row content as before the migration: action `grant` / `credit.adjustment` / `credit.deduction`, resource `credit`, metadata `customer_id`/`amount_cents`/`description` — wire strings frozen. *(walked 2026-08-03 on the W0 PM Walk customer with a timestamp mark: $5 grant → exactly ONE `grant`/`credit` audit row (metadata `amount_cents=500` + `customer_id`) and exactly ONE `customer_credit_ledger` row after the mark — born together; then a $10,000 over-balance adjust → 422 "insufficient balance: available 5.00, deduction 10000.00" and ZERO audit rows of any deduction shape after the mark. The failed attempt left no phantom evidence.)*
- [x] **Background + public flows now leave evidence (ADR-090 PR4)**: (a) let dunning exhaust into cancel_subscription → the sub's `cancel` audit row appears with `canceled_by: dunning`, actor System — identical shape to an operator cancel; (b) engine-relief void (or operator void) → one `void` invoice row carrying `status_before` and, when credits were consumed, `credits_reversed_cents`; (c) a reconciler/engine-issued credit note → one `credit_note.issued` row (no doubles vs the operator path); (d) a Stripe refund flip to `failed` → an `update` credit_note row with metadata `action: refund_status_changed`, `refund_status: failed` — a stale redelivery adds NO row; (e) `POST /v1/tenants` → the NEW tenant's own log opens with a `create` tenant row attributed to the platform key (recorded in the LIVE plane — switch the dashboard to live mode to see it); (f) hosted-invoice Pay click → an `update` invoice row `action: hosted_checkout_started` attributed to the **customer**; payment-update link click → `payment_update_checkout_started` on the customer, and if the Stripe create fails a paired `payment_update_checkout_restored` row keeps the log truthful. *(walked 2026-08-03. (a) FRESH — no walk had EVER exhausted dunning into cancel (0 rows on census, every prior escalation used other final actions), so a purpose-built fixture ran it: cardless clock customer on a `final_action=cancel_subscription` policy, one advance rode cycle-close → no_payment_method enrollment → reminder ladder → `escalated/retries_exhausted` → sub `canceled`, and the audit row is exactly the shape: `cancel`/`subscription`, `actor_type=system`, `canceled_by=dunning`, sim-stamped. (b–f) HISTORY, by census with spot-read metadata: void rows carry `status_before` (I4/D2 walks); `credit_note.issued` ×83 engine rows; `refund_status_changed→failed` ×3 (C3 walks) with the stale-redelivery-adds-nothing half proven live on 2026-08-02; tenant `create` rows in the live plane for both bootstraps (TrialWalk 08-02 + 08-03); `hosted_checkout_started` ×11 and `payment_update_checkout_started` ×2, all `actor_type=customer`. The failure-pairing `payment_update_checkout_restored` row is CI-pinned against real Postgres (`intx_audit_integration_test.go` asserts exactly one row from `RestoreAudited`) — forcing a live Stripe-create failure would mean breaking the tenant's provider config, the standing forbidden fixture.)*
- [x] **Every mutating route records the truth, not a guess (ADR-090)**: (a) delete a meter's **pricing rule** → the audit row reads *deleted meter_pricing_rule* with the rule's id and its true `meter_id` (pre-fix this was recorded as "deleted meter {meter_id}" — a fabricated row); deleting a nonexistent rule (404) adds NO row; (b) create a customer → one `create customer` row with `external_id` + `email_set` (never the raw email); (c) create a manual invoice (with and without line items) → one `create invoice` row each; add a line item → an `update invoice` row with `action: line_item_added`; (d) create / void / retry-refund a credit note → one row each (`create`, `void`, `update`+`action: refund_retried`); a re-drive that gets the same refund status back from Stripe **still adds a row**, carrying `status_changed: false` — the operator's retry hit Stripe, so it IS the fact, and suppressing it would erase a real money-path action. (Contrast the WEBHOOK redelivery in the box above, which mutates nothing and correctly adds no row.) (e) upsert / delete a provider-cost rate → one row each; (f) instantiate a recipe → one `create recipe` row listing what it installed (plan/meter/rule ids); **re-apply the same recipe (idempotent no-op) → NO second row**; (g) trigger a billing run → one `run billing` row with `invoices_created`, distinct from the per-invoice `finalize` rows. *(walked 2026-08-03, all seven: (a) FRESH — created + deleted a throwaway rule on a throwaway meter: `delete meter_pricing_rule vlx_mpr_d9o51njm…` with true `meter_id=vlx_mtr_d9o51fjm…` in metadata, and the nonexistent-id 404 added zero rows; (b) HISTORY — today's creates carry exactly `{"email_set": true, "external_id": "w0-pm-walk"}`, and a leak sweep across ALL customer rows for raw addresses returned 0; (c) HISTORY — creates with `line_item_count`, plus a real `line_item_added` update row from the #697 rail walk; (d) HISTORY — CN create/void rows, AND both retry shapes on record: `refund_retried failed→succeeded status_changed=true` (2026-07-30 recovery) and `failed→failed status_changed=false` (2026-08-02 stuck-CN re-drive) — the operator-action-is-the-fact rule, live; (e) FRESH — `update provider_cost` + `delete provider_cost`, one row each with full rate metadata; (f) FRESH — one `create recipe` row listing installed plan/meter ids, re-apply left count at 1; (g) FRESH — `POST /v1/billing/run` → one `run billing` row `{"action":"cycle_run_triggered","invoices_created":0,"failures":0}`, no finalize rows minted.)*
- [x] **Voiding a credit note is race-safe**: a `void` that loses to a concurrent `issue` returns a conflict naming the note's actual state — it does NOT report success (pre-fix it blind-wrote `voided` over an issued note whose credit had already been granted). *(walked 2026-08-03 at the guard level: `Void` re-reads the note and refuses on BOTH terminal shapes with state-naming messages — "credit note is already voided" and "cannot void an issued credit note — issued credit notes are final financial documents" — so a void that loses to a concurrent issue reports the note's ACTUAL state rather than success. The blind-write the box describes is structurally gone: the guard reads the persisted status inside the operation instead of trusting the caller's view. A true two-goroutine collision fixture is CI territory (the money-path playbook's collision-test class) rather than a hand-walk.)*
- [x] **A new mutating route cannot ship un-audited**: add any `r.Post/Put/Patch/Delete` to the router and run `go test ./internal/api` → FAILS, naming the route, until it is declared in `internal/api/audit_routes.go` as `explicit` (it emits its own row) or `exempt(reason)` (a closed enum: `non_mutating_preview`, `machine_ingest`, `system_endpoint`, `webhook_owned`). Deleting a route while leaving its registry entry behind fails the same test (stale entry). This two-way diff walks the LIVE chi route table — it cannot drift from what is actually mounted. *(walked 2026-08-03 by the box's own prescription: added `r.Post("/v1/p2-walk-phantom-route", …)` → `TestAuditRouteRegistry_MatchesLiveRoutes` FAILED naming it, with the teaching text about exempt() un-auditing a route forever; reverted → green.)*
- [x] **A route that silently stops emitting is reported, not papered over**: an operator action that commits and writes no audit row increments `velox_audit_uncovered_mutation_total{route=…}` and logs `UNCOVERED MUTATION` with the route pattern — where the deleted catch-all would have invented a row guessed from the URL. (CI-locked: `internal/api`'s `TestMain` fails the package if any e2e-exercised route reports one.) *(walked 2026-08-03 as a negative control: after two full days of walk traffic across every domain, `/metrics` carries ZERO `velox_audit_uncovered_mutation_total` series — no route has silently stopped emitting — and the CI gate (`audit_coverage_e2e_test.go` TestMain) runs on every PR.)*
- [x] **Forgotten audit wiring fails at boot**: removing any `SetAuditLogger` line for an in-tx-audited service from router.go panics at startup via `audit.MustWired` — exercised by every CI job that constructs the router (golden-path). The source-pin test (`TestMustWired_CoversEveryAuditedComponent`) covers the complementary drift: a NEW SetAuditLogger wiring line that forgets to join the MustWired list fails in CI. *(walked 2026-08-03: the pin test exists (`internal/api/mustwired_pin_test.go`) and golden-path — which boots the real router — has run green on every PR this campaign; the boot-panic path is the mechanism those runs exercise.)*
- [x] **Saved-card checkout completion leaves customer-attributed evidence**: complete a hosted-invoice payment ticking "save card" (or a payment-update link setup) → an `update` customer row with metadata `action: payment_setup_completed`, card brand/last4, attributed to the **customer**; a `checkout.session.completed` replay for a torn-down or cross-mode customer writes NO row (zero-row UPDATE ⇒ no fabricated evidence). *(walked 2026-08-03 across 25 recorded completions, and the attribution splits EXACTLY by purpose: `velox_purpose=hosted_invoice_pay` and `payment_update_token` → `actor_type=customer` (the customer acted), while `portal_add_payment_method` → `actor_type=system` (the operator minted it) — including the two cards this walk attached. Rows carry `action=payment_setup_completed` with card brand/last4 and no PII. The torn-down/cross-mode replay half is the zero-row-UPDATE guard, CI-covered.)*
- [x] **Dashboard actions are attributed to the operator, not "System"**: any action taken from the signed-in dashboard records `actor_type=user` with the operator's user id (Actor column shows the user, not "System"). Only genuine background work (engine auto-cancel, scheduler) records `actor_type=system`. API-key (Bearer) callers still record `actor_type=api_key` with the key name. *(walked 2026-08-03 — one screen shows the split live (`p2-audit-log-list.png`): the session-driven grant/clock-create/advance rows all read `owner@walkthroug…` in the Actor column, while the SAME advance's engine consequences (Finalized VLX-000071/72, Canceled p2-dc-sub, the no-PM setup-link email) all read "System". Never a raw `vlx_usr_…` id, never "System" on an operator click.)*
- [x] **`request_id` is server-minted, never client-chosen**: make any mutating call with a forged header — `curl -X POST … -H 'X-Request-Id: forged-correlation-id'`. The resulting audit row's `request_id` (visible in the exported CSV's **Request ID** column, and on `GET /v1/audit-log`) is a server id prefixed `req_`, NOT `forged-correlation-id` — and the `Velox-Request-Id` response header carries that same server value. (The expanded row in the dashboard does not surface request_id today; the CSV and the API are where you read it.) chi's stock `middleware.RequestID` honoured the inbound header verbatim, which let a caller choose the correlation id recorded on their own audit rows — correlation evidence an adversary can write is not evidence. *(automated: `TestRequestID_IgnoresClientSuppliedHeader`)* *(walked live 2026-08-03: a credit grant POSTed with `X-Request-Id: forged-correlation-id` → response header `Velox-Request-Id: req_d9o5hlb…` and the resulting audit row's `request_id` is that SAME server value — the forged string appears nowhere.)*
- [x] **Append-only is enforced by TWO independent layers (migration 0150)**: as the **runtime role** (`velox_app`), `UPDATE` / `DELETE` / `TRUNCATE` on `audit_log` fail with `permission denied for table audit_log` (SQLSTATE **42501**) — the write grants are revoked, so the statement never reaches a trigger. As the **table owner / superuser** (which still holds those privileges), the same three statements fail with `audit_log is append-only…` (SQLSTATE **P0001**) — the triggers hold independently, exactly as before. Rows remain intact either way; before 0150 the triggers were the ONLY barrier. *(automated: `TestAuditLog_AppendOnlyIsTwoIndependentLayers`)* *(walked live 2026-08-03 on the walk DB: `SET ROLE velox_app; UPDATE audit_log …` → verbatim "permission denied for table audit_log"; the same UPDATE as superuser → verbatim "audit_log is append-only; UPDATE is not permitted" with the retention HINT — both layers observed firing independently, rows intact.)*
- [x] **Bulk data egress is audited (ADR-090 §7)**: exporting customers / invoices / subscriptions / usage events / the audit log each writes one `export` row (amber, medium severity) — the only READ Velox records. See FLOW EX (EX5–EX10) for the full contract, incl. fail-closed. *(walked 2026-08-03 for the audit-log instance: every export this walk ran wrote exactly one `export`/`audit_log` row carrying format, filters (incl. clock scope when filtered), and the generated filename; the wider per-resource contract + fail-closed lives in FLOW EX as referenced.)* **Known, deliberate loss**: ordinary paginated list reads are NOT audited — an operator who walks `GET /v1/customers?limit=100&offset=…` copies the same data and `audit_log` will not show it. What it answers is "did anyone take the one-click bulk export". Closure trigger: a DP/auditor asking for PII-read evidence → an access-log-derived read trail, not a row per GET.
- [x] **Append-only is TRUNCATE-proof — at whichever layer you hit first**: as the **runtime role** (`velox_app`, even with `app.bypass_rls='on'`), `TRUNCATE audit_log` fails with `permission denied for table audit_log` (SQLSTATE **42501**) — migration 0150 revoked the grant, so the statement never reaches a trigger. As the **table owner / superuser**, which still holds the privilege, the same statement fails with `audit_log is append-only; TRUNCATE is not permitted` (SQLSTATE **P0001**) — the trigger holds independently. Rows remain intact either way. (Before 0150 the app role reached the trigger and saw the P0001 message; expecting that message from `velox_app` today is expecting a barrier that has been superseded by a stricter one.) *(walked live 2026-08-03: `SET ROLE velox_app; TRUNCATE audit_log` → "permission denied for table audit_log"; as superuser → "audit_log is append-only; TRUNCATE is not permitted" — each layer at its own station, rows intact.)*
- [x] **No customer PII in audit metadata**: change a customer's email, upsert a billing profile with a tax ID, send an invoice, send a setup link → the audit rows' expanded Details show `email_changed`/`tax_id_set` flags and ids — never the raw email address or tax ID (append-only log + GDPR erasure). The email outbox keeps the actual delivery addresses. *(walked 2026-08-03 by adversarial sweep: 241 flag-carrying rows (`email_set`/`email_changed`/`tax_id_set`) with ids only; both `setup_link_sent` emitters (operator handler + engine adapter) write `session_id`/`invoice_id`/`trigger` with explicit GDPR comments. HONEST RESIDUE: a regex sweep found 31 rows whose metadata carries a raw `"to"` address — ALL dated ≤2026-06-12, from a pre-fix emitter version; the log is append-only so the historical rows survive by design. Every row written since carries no address. Not a live defect; recorded so the next auditor isn't surprised by the old rows.)*
- [x] **Operator rows show the operator's name**: dashboard-driven rows render the user's display name or email in the Actor column (never the raw `user` enum or a bare `vlx_usr_…` id). *(walked 2026-08-03: dashboard-driven rows render `owner@walkthrough.local` in the Actor column — never the raw `user` enum, never a bare `vlx_usr_…` id (the id is present only as the cell's hover title). Verified on the same screen where engine rows read "System", so the two are visibly distinguishable.)*
- [x] **Humanized rows**: the action badge reads "Subscription Item Updated", not `subscription.item_updated`; metadata labels case acronyms correctly ("IP Address", not "Ip Address"); structured metadata values (e.g. the settings `changed` map) render as JSON, not `[object Object]`; item add/remove/update rows on a subscription carry the sub's code as the label; expanded Resource ID has a copy button. *(walked 2026-08-03 on the fresh fixture rows: titles read "Granted credits to P2 request-id probe" / "Canceled p2-dc-sub" (the sub's code as label) / "Advanced test clock \"P2 dunning-cancel walk\"" — no dotted strings anywhere on screen; the expanded row labels "IP address" correctly, renders Plan Ids as a JSON array (`["vlx_pln_…"]`), and the Resource ID carries its copy button. - [x] **Membership + auth rows are humanized, and a removal reads as destructive**: invite a teammate, have them accept, then remove them; sign out and back in; toggle test/live. `/audit-log` renders "Invited sam@…", "Joined the team (sam@…)", "Revoked a team invitation", "Removed a team member", "Signed in", "Signed out", "Switched to live mode" — never a raw dotted string like `member.joined`. The **member.removed** row carries the destructive red left border (it revokes the target's sessions), like `revoke` / `delete`. *(automated: `cd web-v2 && npm test` → `tests/auditVocabulary.test.ts`)* *(walked 2026-08-03 from rows the campaign minted: `member.invited` ×2, `member.joined` ×1, `member.invite_revoked` ×1, `login` ×76, `logout` ×18, `mode_changed` ×27 — plus password-reset pairs from today's credential rotation. The vocabulary maps each to human copy ("Invited …", "Joined the team (…)", "Revoked a team invitation", "Removed a team member", "Signed in", "Signed out", "Switched to live/test mode") with no dotted string reaching the screen, and `member.removed` sits in HIGH_SEVERITY beside void/cancel/delete/revoke — the destructive accent the box requires, with the reason recorded in code ("it strips a person's access and revokes their live sessions"). `member.removed` itself has never fired in this tenant; the rendering + severity are pinned by `web-v2/tests/auditVocabulary.test.ts`.)*
- [x] **Test-clock rows deep-link**: expand a `test_clock` audit row (created / advanced / deleted) → its **View** link opens `/test-clocks/<id>`.
